# macOS implementation contracts

### 2026-10-30 — lead — Voice Lab merged on both platforms

- Squash-merged track/sol-mac-voicelab at 03e43989 (W1-W11) and track/sol-win-voicelab at 3f5c3ea6 (c1-c7) together, so the shared request goldens, quote mutations and pause-fit vectors match on both Cores.
- Voice Lab stays behind the beta opt-in: Release needs `enabled && betaNoticeAccepted`; DEBUG needs `enabled && (betaNoticeAccepted || internalBuild)`, for exposure and generation alike.
- Merge rule for transcript accessibility: an accepted replacement span reads its replacement words, then "playing", then "AI modified", and never inherits the dormant source words' cut state. Replacement spans list their source word ids, so a collapsed cut run is identified as a span with ids that is not an accepted replacement.
- The caption gallery audit keeps main's eager rows and measured convergence; W11's run-loop layout turn replaces `Task.yield()` while the gallery is not ready.
- design.md §13 now carries the shipped room-tone refusal ("… Clean audio is not available in this build. Re-record this sentence instead."); [Clean Audio…] returns when Clean audio (M2) ships.
- Push gates: hosted CI on the merged head and the native window gates (appSelfTest, documentScene, demoWindow, voiceRecipeWorkflow, settingsLifecycle, saveBoundary) on an idle, unlocked console.
- Owed to 0.1.0: the Windows Voice Lab App (track sol-win-voicelab-ui and its editor-side track), native Windows evidence (ORT parity, then the A8 flip), and the Mac screen-reader headphones caption that Windows R4 introduces.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W11 hosted gallery audit

W10 CI 41027662313 also failed the window-free gallery's undo-at-360 check. Its settle loop incorrectly treated `Task.yield()` as a render-loop turn: the task may resume before AppKit's coalesced SwiftUI/layout work. The audit now awaits the main run loop's before-waiting phase, after layout observers, before comparing the next snapshot. The two-second bound, request-consumption and full-tile/header/ring geometry assertions are unchanged. Failures include the expected/actual style and complete measured snapshot in their names, which survive CI's temporary evidence cleanup. A headless test proves a before-waiting layout observer has fired before settlement resumes; another pins failure diagnostics and rejects an unsettled matching style. This is audit scheduling, not a relaxation of gallery visibility.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W11 recovery accessibility

Every per-voice and per-operation action reads `<action> <voice name>, created <d MMMM yyyy>` using the existing fixed English month table. Actions include Delete voice, Retry deletion, Remove from VoxClip only, Retry saving consent record, Retry, Remove unfinished voice and `I've checked my ElevenLabs account — stop looking`. Visible titles and Cancel-default confirmations are unchanged. When rows have the same name and formatted creation date, append `, voice ID ending <last six voice ID characters>`, or `, attempt <one-based pending-operation ordinal>` for operations without a voice ID. Missing/blank names read `Unnamed voice` with the same identity suffix. Pending entries are labelled accessibility containers with separate child actions, using this same name/date/identity context. Headless tests pin exact labels for two and three same-name/same-day saved voices and operations, including known remote IDs and an unnamed operation. The source guard proves every action actually uses the tested context and fails against the W10 Settings source.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W11 first paid transport commit

This supersedes W10's pre-transport `started` handshake as the retirement boundary. The handshake only validates preparation; it does not clear rows or retire scratch. The first successful, revision/consent-guarded transport resume commits replacement on the MainActor, synchronously with the live cap decision and cancellation. Only then are the old rows/selection/audition replaced and Core retires prior scratch/snapshot leases. No failed validation, declined sheet or cancellation before resume retires them. No progress row is published for a never-started run.

A declined pre-dispatch confirmation retains the exact immutable whole-run quote, displayed price, previous selection and playing audition. The next click rechecks current spending and asks again. Core unapproves the unstarted run but keeps its pending preparation; TTS restores a claimed quote only when transport provably never resumed, the original monotonic deadline is retained, and that run still owns approval. A started or ambiguous transport can never restore its claim. Explicit cancellation instead invalidates the reservation and automatically prepares a fresh quote (or shows Retry). Deterministic App tests exercise a cap crossed after the preparation handshake, decline/retry, approved first dispatch, and cancellation racing its cap read. Core tests exercise the same first-transport race with a real fitted scratch/snapshot, a playing fake-backend audition, an actual ledger mutation and stub HTTP; declined runs send nothing and the same quote can subsequently be approved, numbered from Take 1.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W10 accepted pre-open directory retention

An open failure immediately after exclusive mkdir has no pinned fd/identity and intentionally leaves its empty operation directory and intent while the owner lives. The next sweep after owner death reclaims it using **empty-only rmdir**, never emptying an identity-less directory. A deterministic post-mkdir/pre-open failure checkpoint verifies retention while live and reclamation after death. This is accepted for 0.1.0, not a cleanup gate left owed.

W10 does not change the shipped room-tone refusal or add a Clean Audio action. `VoiceLabGeometryError.roomToneUnavailable` shows "This take has no half-second pause to copy the room sound from. Clean audio removes background sound from the whole take, so new words can blend in. Clean audio is not available in this build. Re-record this sentence instead." The lead updates design §13 at merge.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W10 identity-less creation recovery

This supersedes W8's region-only allowance for uncertain **creation** discovery: without a creator key fingerprint, recovery performs no discovery, never trusts an empty listing, and never abandons automatically. It durably marks manual review and shows "Recovery needs the ElevenLabs key that started this voice." Settings offers "I've checked my ElevenLabs account — stop looking", with Cancel the default confirmation. Only that explicit action writes `remoteStateUnknown: true` and finishes with a retained abandoned operation tombstone (including available audit hashes and deletion timestamp); crash recovery finishes that local-only cleanup without provider calls. No consent/authorization is restored. Legacy **deletion** retains region-only matching and matched-region 404 handling. Core tests cover repeated restarts beyond 24 hours under a different account with zero discovery/deletion calls, and faults at the stop action's durable boundaries. A headless App test exercises the explicit confirmation, unknown-state tombstone and lack of provider-consent side effects. Normal discovery tests now seed creator-bound operations rather than relying on legacy identity-less fixtures.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W10 live cap decisions

The immutable whole-run quote is not a snapshot of the user's monthly cap or other actions' spending. Generation rereads the ledger and then the current cap setting before launch and at every TTS/Scribe transport boundary. After any confirmation suspension it recomputes the decision again: a newly crossed cap, unknown price or S14 confirmation cannot be silently approved by an older decision. The approved copy is bound to this run; changed confirmation copy asks again, while identical already-approved copy does not. Unreadable spend with a configured cap fails closed. Quote/revision/consent and task cancellation are rechecked after the cap read and after the sheet; the final transport start is synchronous with that validation on the MainActor and retains Core's voice-use/revision permit. Headless regressions mutate an actual ledger between preparation/click, during a suspended sheet and immediately before transport; another declines the real approval callback and proves previous takes/selection and automatic requoting survive.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W10 replacement cancellation

Replacement cancellation retains previous rows, selection, scratch/snapshot leases and audition until the approved launch commits on the MainActor. The run's throwing `started` handshake rejects a stale/cancelled launch before retiring leases. Cancellation and that handshake are serialized on the MainActor; cleanup cancels the old execution with `keepingTakes`, waits for it to finish, then automatic quote preparation makes Generate Again/Try Another usable (or exposes Retry on failure). Deterministic App tests cancel before approval, during approval cancellation and at a suspended launch; a real Core take-run test rejects launch and proves the scratch and playing audition survive until session invalidation. No sleeps or live requests.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W9 native Rewrite menu self-test

The old native menu check expected the retired pre-W6 opt-in sheet while Voice Lab was off. The central beta gate intentionally hides Rewrite and blocks both its action and hidden shortcut while off; in DEBUG it permits enabled internal builds without a beta notice, and Release still requires the notice. The native check refreshes Edit-menu visibility, invokes the actual action and proves no sheet, Rewrite session or opening task appears while off, then checks the enabled selected-passage route. Failures print labelled expected/actual state. Headless App checks cover the actual menu mutation gate in all enabled/notice combinations, plus busy-run and missing-media refusals. No production menu policy changes.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W9 room-tone copy self-test

The native room-tone refusal expectation predates W6's capability limitation. Product copy correctly says Clean audio is unavailable in this build and offers re-recording instead; do not remove that qualification or advertise a non-existent cleaning client. Native and headless App checks now assert the complete exact refusal, not just substrings. A native mismatch prints the full expected and actual copy.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W9 verification approval self-test

The native verification check previously had no statement WAV and asserted synchronously, although verification now prepares a local quote asynchronously. Its DEBUG-only fixture stages a synthetic 16 kHz statement and an injected client whose key provider records and refuses credential access. The fixture temporarily selects that client's default region so `services()` cannot replace it with a real client for a user's configured region. `request` returns its preparation task for deterministic awaiting; existing UI callers still discard it. The check proves a busy, unverified/no-confirmation state before preparation and an idle, unverified/Verify-confirmation state afterward, with zero credential requests (therefore no paid dispatch). Both states print expected/actual on failure. A headless App regression runs all twelve native consent logic checks from each of the five configured regions, restores settings and speaker prerequisites, and never opens a window, records audio or contacts a provider. The unavailable-speaker check clears stale errors and explicitly requests creation before asserting refusal.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W9 attestation self-test prerequisites

The native attestation check stages an installed, calibrated English speaker catalog entry without loading a model or using a microphone. It checks all eight combinations, allowing recording only with all three attestations. The former fixture omitted the speaker pre-gate added in W6. Headless App tests cover the same eight combinations alongside the existing unavailable/uncalibrated-speaker refusal tests. Diagnostic comparisons retain expected and actual values in a failed result's name, so the native harness prints both in its failure log without changing the report schema.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W8 same-name recovery parity

The Mac's existing uncertainty predicate already treats every same-name candidate not selected by the nonce or valid missing-label creation window as uncertain, including dated candidates outside that window. Keep this rule unchanged. Added regressions cover an out-of-window candidate alone and alongside an exact nonce match: neither deletes anything, both durably require manual review, and an empty listing after restarting cannot abandon the operation or clear that review obligation.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W8 creator-bound recovery discovery

Recovery already checked the operation's region and creator fingerprint before discovery. Now every discovery page additionally binds the fingerprint to the exact key used by transport, closing key changes between the initial check and dispatch or between pages. Recheck the selected region before trusting results. A mismatch leaves the durable operation pending and Settings shows "Recovery needs the ElevenLabs key that started this voice." No empty result from a different creator can abandon an operation. Legacy operations still match on their recorded region only. Deterministic tests change keys before the initial check, before first dispatch and before a later page; all retain the obligation beyond its 24-hour window without deletion or abandonment.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W8 pre-receipt initialization failure

Immediately after exclusive mkdir, open the operation directory with `O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW` and capture its identity with fstat before writing the receipt. An ordinary failure before a durable receipt only attempts rmdir after comparing the open fd and current path identities; it never empties a non-empty directory. A failure after open but before initial identity assignment obtains the identity from that same pinned fd in cleanup. A replacement or non-empty directory and its intent survive. After a complete receipt is journalled, the existing owned-directory cleanup applies. Deterministic `opened` and `directory` fault points cover both pre-receipt stages, plus path replacement and unexpected contents.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W8 intent-only recovery

An intent proves a planned name, not exclusive creation. With a dead/reused owner and no valid identity receipt, recovery only attempts `rmdir` (`unlinkat` with `AT_REMOVEDIR`) on the canonical real directory pinned with `O_NOFOLLOW`; it never unlinks entries. A non-empty intent-only directory, including one containing only an incomplete `.operation.json` or VoxClip-named partials, and its intent are retained with a redacted warning. An absent directory drops the intent. Identity-bearing receipts retain the existing descriptor-confined cleanup protocol.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W8 automatic replacement quotes

This supersedes W7's unpriced preparation click. On run completion and voice/model/text/consent changes, prepare a fresh immutable whole-three-take quote automatically, using local pricing and free discovery only. Keep the previous rows, selection and scratch/audition leases until an approved replacement actually starts. While preparing, both Generate Again and Try Another have plain disabled titles; once ready, both show the known estimate before the click, including estimates that need confirmation. One click executes a known below-S14 quote or opens confirmation otherwise. Declining approval preserves rows and selection. Preparation errors remain inline with an enabled Retry that prepares only; it never dispatches a paid request. Changed inputs invalidate approval and acceptance of old takes, synchronously invalidate the run's dispatch revision, and cancel the old run without destroying audition leases. `cancel(keepingTakes: true)` preserves these leases; normal cancel/session end still invalidates them.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W7 Windows command handoff

This entry supersedes the older W5 statement that `VoiceLabEditCommand` is DEBUG-only. The command and editor actions ship in Release behind the central beta gate. The pure `applying(to:)` transition performs no I/O and never deletes media. `EditorModel.applyVoiceLab` additionally requires an editable document, the central Rewrite gate, no export and no AI run. Any accompanying local edit commands are validated and committed in the same undo group; a failed transition changes nothing.

| Variant | Payload | Preconditions and transition | Inverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `accept` | `VoiceLabSegment`, `assets: [VoiceLabAsset]`, `voice: VoiceLabVoiceReference` | Validate/upgrade the project to v3 (only v1 or segment-free v2 can upgrade). Require an unused segment ID, an in-range selected candidate and a valid segment/recipe. A supplied existing voice must equal the stored reference; a new reference must be valid, active, ElevenLabs, uniquely bound and have a trimmed 1–60-scalar name. All unrejected own-voice candidates require an active matching reference. Identical existing asset records are reused; conflicting IDs or duplicate new asset IDs fail. Add the reference, new assets and segment, then validate the whole project. Action: `Accept Voice Lab take`. | Restore the exact pre-accept project snapshot, including schema version, catalog/reference contents, segments, local edits and `modifiedAt`; keep all published files. Redo restores the accepted snapshot, not another provider call. |
| `setEnabled` | `segmentID: String`, `enabled: Bool` | Require an existing segment; set its enabled flag and validate the whole project (including enabled-segment geometry). `false` restores original source playback without deleting the recipe/assets. `true` re-enables the stored recipe. Actions: `Turn off Voice Lab` / `Turn on Voice Lab`. | Restore the prior enabled flag as part of the full pre-command snapshot, not a blind boolean toggle. Redo restores the post-command snapshot. |
| `delete` | `segmentID: String` | Require an existing segment; remove only that segment and validate the whole project. Retain catalog assets and voice references; never delete a clone or paid media. Action: `Delete Voice Lab segment`. | Restore the removed segment at its original position and all other pre-command state from the snapshot. Redo restores the deletion snapshot. |
| `chooseCandidate` | `segmentID: String`, `index: Int` (zero-based) | Require an existing segment, an in-range unrejected candidate, and its catalogued asset. For v3 require its recipe, then copy selected index, output duration, audio, words, provenance and QA from that recipe and validate the whole project. Existing accepted takes remain usable after voice deletion; do not re-require active provider consent. Legacy selection updates audio asset/trim/stretch. Action: `Choose Voice Lab take`. | Restore the previous selection and all mirrored recipe fields from the full snapshot, not just the index. Redo restores the chosen snapshot. |

Every changed command is one native UndoManager step. Undo and redo preserve their exact stored `modifiedAt`, project/asset references and document change count on both clean and already-dirty documents. A new edit gets a new timestamp; undo/redo do not. The title continues to follow Rename/Save As rather than undo history. No-op transitions create no undo step. Undo-history and open/unsaved-document asset references remain protected from retention cleanup.

**Generate Again / Try Another.** These are whole-run generation, not `chooseCandidate` and not a single-take replacement API. First click prepares a fresh immutable three-take quote and displays its run price on that button, retaining old rows, audition and selection. Next click executes a current known quote below S14's $0.50 threshold (unless the monthly cap requires confirmation); unknown, at/above-threshold or cap-crossing prices ask for approval. Cancelling keeps old takes and selection exactly. Only an approved run's actual start replaces the rows and restarts at Take 1. A changed quote cannot inherit approval. The single-take `takes`/`firstIndex` API remains deferred.

**`pauseFitNote(_:)`.** Pure cross-platform formatting: prefix `pauses `; locale `en_US_POSIX`; choose `+` for input >= 0, including negative zero, otherwise U+2212 `−`; format the absolute magnitude with `%.1f`, then append ` s`. Choose the sign before rounding: -0.01 gives `pauses −0.0 s`, -0.0 gives `pauses +0.0 s`. Never use the current locale's decimal separator. `RewriteGenerationTests.pauseNotesHaveStableCrossPlatformSigns` pins the edge cases; `test_command_handoff.py` keeps the variant/payload/precondition/inverse table aligned with the production enum.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W7 replacement-run approval

- Generate Again and Try Another prepare a fresh immutable quote for all three takes. Preparing or cancelling approval preserves the existing rows, audition leases and exact selection. The next click executes a known under-threshold quote, otherwise asks for confirmation. Only starting the approved run replaces the rows; its numbering starts at Take 1. A stale displayed quote cannot approve a different replacement quote. The single-take retry API remains deferred.
- Regression coverage: `RewriteGenerationTests.replacementQuoteKeepsTakesUntilApproved` (known/unknown price, selection, cancellation and start) and `VoiceLabTakeRunTests.freshWholeRunQuotePreservesAuditionUntilReplacementStarts` (real mocked run, private scratch lifetime and numbering).
- Release authorization probes use the production `authorized(for:proof:)` and full speaker-proof initializer signatures, with typed store/record/embedder arguments. Every negative probe requires its specific private/internal/fileprivate diagnostic; signature or import errors alone are not evidence. Same-source positive controls compile those calls where access permits, including the DEBUG injection initializer. The source-wide initializer scan remains in force. `test_release_boundary.py` tests the diagnostic assertions and real signatures.
- Exposure and generation both use `VoiceLabGate.rewriteEnabled`: `enabled && (noticeAccepted || internalBuild)`, with `internalBuild` true only in DEBUG. Generation adds provider consent, region and current-input checks, not another beta-notice rule. `RewriteGenerationTests.exposureAndGenerationShareTheBetaGate` exercises the real session alongside the exposure gate.
- Rejecting the selected take selects the highest-ranked remaining eligible row using the run's shared score/index ordering, resets its video choice, and announces `Rejected Take N. Take M selected.` A changed-length fallback still requires an explicit video choice. Only an empty eligible set enters `nonePassed`; rejecting another row preserves selection. `rejectionSelectsNextEligibleTakeByRank` covers these paths.
- Operation creation first durably writes a parent intent (`.voxclip-op-<uuid>.intent.json`: planned `op-<uuid>` name and pid/kernel start time), then exclusively creates the 0700 directory, then durably writes its identity receipt `.operation.json` inside it. Together these form the two-stage operation journal. Recovery requires a dead/reused owner. Without a valid identity receipt it accepts only the exact canonical directory name, an owned real 0700 directory pinned with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and no entries except the VoxClip identity receipt itself (no audio is written before initialization completes). Missing directories drop their intents; ordinary initialization failures remove the still-identical directory immediately. Valid identity receipts retain all previous replacement/path-race protections; old identity-bearing operation journals remain recoverable. Deterministic persisted crash fixtures and injected failure tests cover both creation gaps, partial identity writes, live/unknown/reused owners, symlinks and foreign entries.
- Consent-verification WAV duration uses explicitly typed byte/sample/conversion/rate/seconds intermediates for Xcode 26. `test_compiler_shapes.py` guards the split against reintroducing nested mixed arithmetic; W7 also runs the 300 ms expression checker and manually scans all W6/W7 changed Swift files.

### 2026-10-30 — sol-mac-voicelab — W6 panel, shipping opt-in and review fixes

- This entry supersedes the W5 staging and cleanup descriptions below. R0 checked CI `40976398364`: its failure was the window-free caption-gallery reveal, fixed by `c7396cbc` with eager two-column catalogue layout. The earlier canonical portrait-counter CLI stall did not recur in the fresh Release E2E run; no timeout, retries or acceptance thresholds were raised. R0 completed the previously interrupted Release, Media and CaptionParity verification. All evidence for this round is under `/tmp/voxclip-sol-mac-voicelab/w6/`; native window tests and paid/live calls are not part of it.
- W5 review fixes: `adc263ee` restores the exact project, including `modifiedAt`, and native change counts on undo/redo for clean and already-dirty documents. `aeaba58f` persists each retention observation, rejects backward clocks/restarts and wall jumps more than 24 hours beyond monotonic elapsed time, and rechecks references immediately before deletion. `fbadb045` plus `b66b89a1` derives export AI disclosure from enabled scope segments independently of asset preflight. `f4d302b2` maps interior clip starts to the leading edge of an accepted replacement and interior ends to its trailing edge; undo/redo restores the source boundaries.
- Accepted transcript accessibility reads the replacement words, optional playing state, then **AI modified**. It never inherits the dormant source words' cut state. The `wordAccessibility` harness builds real accepted-segment states and checks all eight readings; `spoken()` remains non-public. The polish track must preserve this rule when merging its transcript work.
- Consent safety: `ef25443e` gives every new enrollment a fresh record ID after success or recovery, refuses withdrawal of an already-active record, and preserves malformed non-pending records. The reopening-then-cancelling regression preserves the active clone and its statement. `d880be75` moves reconciliation off settings/import/quit, skips beta-off startup with no records or obligations, and cancels on quit. `afc6e2e2` names the selected connector and Rewrite in errors.
- Own-voice authority is a sealed, file-private `VoiceSpeakerMatch` proof, not caller-supplied booleans or reserve/validate callbacks. It binds the exact current record and statement, the calibrated model/language and validated speaker match; the dispatch permit rechecks consent and run revision atomically at transport start. `speakerProofCannotAuthorizeAnotherRecordOrOutliveWithdrawal` covers cross-record use and withdrawal. Release controls reject fabrication from another file in the same module as well as external/package callers.
- `creator` is the optional exact object `{region, keyFingerprint}` in `record.json`; region is a settings raw string and fingerprint is the first 16 lowercase SHA-256 hex characters over key UTF-8 bytes. Creation/deletion journals use `creatorKeyFingerprint`. New records always bind both; legacy records without `creator` match recorded record/journal region only, defaulting to `default`. No key is persisted. Both schema fixtures are validated and malformed/numeric regions, missing/extra properties and non-lowercase fingerprints fail. A changed key/region never dispatches DELETE and its 404 cannot confirm removal. Local-only removal writes `remoteStateUnknown: true` and preserves the audit. These rules supersede the flat optional fingerprint fields in the earlier implementation; no released record used those fields.
- Recovery keeps the best-effort provider label and nonce description, with exact-name + creation-window + owned-clone-category fallback only when the nonce is absent. A conflicting nonce never matches or sends DELETE, and uncertainty remains visible. No legacy-audit migration is added: `missingAuditAndDraftCannotAuthorizeButStillCleansUp` proves a missing audit cannot authorize but cleanup still runs without crashing.
- Conformance: clone multipart repeats **files**, not `files[]`; discovery sends `page_size=100`, skips incomplete name/category rows, preserves paging and maps distinct 403 codes accurately. `eleven_v3` omits and does not quote-bind unsupported stitching context. Shared goldens include the English/Estonian recovery labels and nonce suffix. Windows must mirror `ElevenLabsVoiceLab.cs`'s clone field and replay the new request/disclosure/consent vectors; no Windows source is changed here.
- Enrollment is pre-gated through `VoiceLabGate.canCheckOwnVoice(speakerAvailable:calibratedThreshold:language:)`, before recording and any paid step. Statement verification uses a one-use audio/language-bound quote and the spend ledger; expired creation quotes preserve verified enrollment for requoting. Delete and own-audio confirmations default to Cancel. Created dates use the reviewed fixed month table, not raw ISO strings.
- Cleanup no longer uses filename + mtime or process-start age. Every store/retention partial has an exclusive-creation journal with pid + kernel process start time and device/inode/birth identity. Partials, snapshots, fitted takes and render scratch now live in unique 0700 operation directories, journalled with their owner and directory identity. Abandoned sweeps open each directory with `O_NOFOLLOW`, compare its `fstat` identity, require a provably dead/reused owner, and unlink entries only through that pinned directory fd. They never rename a candidate into quarantine or follow a replaced directory path. The owning lease uses the same fd for explicit release/invalidation; it never recursively deletes a scratch URL. Unknown owners, unjournalled/legacy loose partials, replacement directories and external symlink targets survive. Completed writers retain ownership receipts. Retention remains separately identity-checked and requires the matching receipt, an exclusive store document-activity lease (other windows/processes hold shared leases), and a fresh union of current/unsaved documents and retained undo-history references. All lifetime locks are kernel-released on process death. Deterministic tests cover a live second owner, dead owner, pid reuse, copied-journal directory replacement, file/directory/symlink swaps after recovery opens the directory, scratch/snapshot replacement during invalidation, a second active document lease and a late reference, without sleeps. Complete unlisted paid assets found at startup are permanently preserved, not automatically removed after seven days; this is the accepted W4 orphan limitation, not a license to infer ownership from a name.
- Decoder snapshots and disclosure staging use the same journalled-directory lease. Disclosure builds its MP4 privately and publishes to the caller's staging name with `RENAME_EXCL` only after every fallible preparation step; failure never unlinks the caller's path. Regressions swap the decoder directory, replace the disclosure directory and collide with its destination, including cancellation after copying.
- Completion announcements count every failed take, including two failed attempts before Take 3 fits; the same count is used for length-changed runs. The headless regression covers one/two failures with both fitting and changed-length winners, rather than hard-coding “one failed” in every run.
- Hosted integration handoff: W6 run `40998419245` found Windows request replay counts still expecting 21 request cases and 18 quote mutations, versus the new 24 and 20 shared cases. The Windows port must adopt the new clone field, nonce and v3 vectors; this Mac track does not change Windows sources or weaken the shared cases to make that job pass.
- Release now exposes Settings → Voice Lab even with the switch off. Rewrite menus, context/selection entries, shortcut listing and editor actions all use the central beta gate. Release requires `enabled && betaNoticeAccepted`; DEBUG allows `enabled && (betaNoticeAccepted || internalBuild)` for harness staging. All eight flag/build combinations are tested. Consent notices/provider approval remain separate from detection and generation approval. DEBUG scenes, fake keys/players and publication hooks stay DEBUG-only; real generation/edit wiring ships behind the opt-in.
- The visible panel wires local source extraction, real discovery/run/QA, displayed quotes/monthly cap, S14 cost confirmation, progress/cancellation, native single-choice 36-point take rows, failed rows, best selection and once-only autoplay, audition/reject, default Use Take and return-to-words focus. Acceptance combines any local cut/text-only hunks with the voice segment in one exact-snapshot undo step. Source extraction is bounded, native-rate and revision-reserved before awaiting; stale preparation/publication cannot mutate the project. A click that needs a replacement quote only prepares and displays it; it never silently submits an unseen new price.
- Windows should mirror the pure `RewriteGenerationSession.pauseFitNote(_:)` formatting (`pauses +0.4 s` / Unicode-minus negative). Configured remote local-server connectors show the actual host; only loopback says nothing leaves the Mac. Reviewed Estonian script structure moves its existing stop line to the real end, retains the 150-word check, and adds the four disclosure lines to the user review checklist without rewriting the Estonian wording or nonce list.
- Scope limitations remain explicit: preparation accepts one voiced passage at a time and refuses multiple rather than omitting them. `zoomCover` now passes Core recipe/render preparation, but §10.5's native source-only renderer gate still disables retimed/lip-sync acceptance with a reason. Clean audio, forced alignment, text-to-dialogue and lip-sync provider clients/state machines remain deferred; unavailable controls do not imply implemented clients. Failed-row Try Another currently names/prices a whole three-take run, not a fictitious single replacement; the `takes`/`firstIndex` API shape remains lead-deferred. Null/malformed alignment stops after the first paid result instead of spending on more unusable takes.
- Window-free evidence covers the eight panel states in light/dark at 300/360/560, plus strict right-column width/height audits. The ImageRenderer files are **static snapshots using shared row rendering**, not proof that native ScrollView/List controls painted or accepted keyboard input. Nested-scroll auditing intersects every ancestor viewport and ignores zero-area AX frames; positive-area overflow still fails. The lead still owes states-board panels 4–7, native acceptance/keyboard/focus, actual list/scroll painting, audition/listening, VoiceOver spot checks and the user's own-voice microphone check. Estonian calibration and wording review items 27–29 remain owed; no word-list edits are made.

### 2026-10-30 — lead — ElevenLabs error detail and residency keys

- A live Scribe upload failed with only "ElevenLabs answered 400: the request could not finish". `ElevenLabsTranscriber.providerError(status:data:key:region:)` now also reads the object form of `detail` (`{type, code, message, status}`), not only a string or a list. `UserMessages` shows the redacted reason unless it is the generic text, and the import logs `engine=elevenlabs status=… detail=…` at warn level.
- A 401, or a body whose `type` is `authentication_error` or whose `code` or `status` is `invalid_api_key`, is a key problem at any HTTP status. The Settings key Test keeps transcription §8.5 and mirrors Windows `TestKeyAsync`: it names the status but never echoes the provider's body. A 401 is keyRejected, a 429 is rateLimited, any other status is a generic provider error (no longer "rejected the key" during an outage), and a network failure reads as offline.
- `ElevenLabsRegion.isResidency` is true for EU, India and Singapore. Those hosts serve separate Enterprise data-residency workspaces with their own keys. On a residency region, a key error or a 400 says so and points to Region: Default. The reason is shortened so the hint stays whole inside the 200-character limit. `ElevenLabsRegion.residencyHint` holds the text once; the key Test adds it to a residency 400 or 401. The Settings → Transcription Advanced footer explains the same. Windows mirrors the mapping in `ElevenLabsTranscriber.MapError` (see apps/windows/CONTRACTS.md).
- Tests: `SpeechErrorCopyTests` covers the object detail, residency key errors, the residency 401 and a 300-character reason. Branch CI 40986827640.

Tile targets use ordinary top alignment with their actual painted top included. Horizontal image sizes and the strict native paint/AX assertions remain unchanged.

- Late tile/header geometry is rechecked after a reveal completes, so an early completion cannot permanently ignore a header that arrives after its tile or a later viewport refinement. Only a real user scroll (or the DEBUG gallery-end command) suspends this refinement; restarting or changing the request clears that interruption. Identical target commands remain coalesced.

A new window-free audit runs 53 checks over first/middle/last selections at 300/320/360/560 points, style changes made outside the gallery (the editor's own setStyle, then Undo), actual target/paint top alignment, and interruption of a pending reveal through the production scroll-phase handler. Its tall unattached hosts intentionally need no scrolling: SwiftUI leaves their content offset at zero, so they establish layout and reactive selection, not native scroll acceptance. Native full/1280/300/560 scenes additionally press the real AX radio choices on the main actor (an in-process AX call from another thread waits on SwiftUI's main-thread sync and deadlocks), then change the style through setStyle and Undo; they require first/middle/last/external/undo paint and AX containment, cancellation through layout, and restoration of the scene selection. The full scene also hosts first/last selections in a viewport shorter than a tile and requires a visible ring top, completion and unchanged command count/offset after further layout. These native checks are for the lead to run; the separate gallery-end/fade audit remains. Short viewports complete at top alignment instead of waiting forever for impossible full containment.

The scroll viewport admits three extra points on each horizontal side while preserving image widths and column containment. Project/scope/style and viewport-size changes request a reveal; measured tile/header geometry prefers the group heading when both fit above the fade. A completed reveal stays completed through lazy-view recycling, and real scrolling cancels pending selection movement. Fine Tune keeps its controls at the top instead of jumping to a tile. The DEBUG gallery-end audit now scrolls through the same SwiftUI reader, not raw NSClipView offsets: its lazy document was observed refining from 1335 to 902 points, so restoring an old pixel offset was not a valid semantic restore. The normal Media suite also runs this check, without enabling interactive UI tests. - Lead review fixes, round 2:
  - Stage height: the viewer's 320-point panel reserve is a lead design decision. The stage keeps its §5.1 frame size wherever 320 points remain for the panel, which covers every aspect at 1280×800 and 1440×900. On shorter columns only its height yields, keeping its aspect. The real-height matrix asserts both cases.
  - Gallery fallback: when the AX tree hides the gallery's scroll area, the native bounds check holds the native viewport to the strict column and window bounds and classifies the gallery content against it.
  - Window-free audit: it no longer runs the native divider drag. That drag stays in the UI self-test.

- Lead review fixes: the playback rate stays hidden from accessibility in both viewer layouts, as before the fallback; the time element alone carries the spoken time. The window-free matrix also requires that it measured the real rows: the visible Frame control with its four labels, and exactly one fit candidate for the Frame, transport and (outside Rewrite) Captions rows. Unattached hosts don't vend SwiftUI's virtual accessibility elements, so required accessibility coverage is the native bounds check's. That check now writes evidence for every capture, fails when an editor is showing without its split, and fails on named controls it can't measure rather than dropping them.
- Native acceptance is not inferred from offscreen layout or a locked-session attempt. The invocation records and final report distinguish failures caused by a locked console from passed gates; no system setting or fixture frame counter is changed. The lead owns the design-document updates.
- Round-one verification passed: build, Core 601 tests, Media 283 tests (including the window-free matrix), CaptionParity 4 tests, design generation check, 17 design tests and contrast. Its one fresh 300 ms slow-expression build passed with no slow-expression warnings. Native editor captures and their full SwiftUI AX bounds were still owed because that console was locked; round-two acceptance must come from its own unlocked native evidence, not those earlier attempts.
- Lead fixes before merge (pre-merge review): the Captions switch sits outside the header's ViewThatFits, centred on the heading by a custom alignment, because turning captions off can swap the header form at 300–340 points and a switch inside the candidates was replaced, losing keyboard and VoiceOver focus. The layout audit now requires the same NSSwitch before and after turning captions off at 300/320/340 (and that a form change happened), and the switch centred on the heading at every width and variant; restoring the old placement fails all three identity checks. A marker on the selection ring itself replaces the tautological tile-versus-target comparison: removing the tile's 3-point top reserve now fails all 12 ring checks, and the native selection check requires the ring inside the viewport. The gallery's Picker accessibility representation (design §6.1) places its radio rows by style order, not over the tiles, so native checks require the matching radio button to be selected rather than its frame to sit in the viewport; the misplaced VoiceOver outline is a known follow-up. The native short-viewport check sizes its host per style to leave a 40-point gallery, since a two-row header left none at a fixed 140 points. Native runs also exposed two ways a reveal could stay pending: a final scroll that lands where the view already is produces no geometry change, so a pending reveal is now re-checked on the next main-queue turn (at most 8 times per command); and a lazy grid's first scroll to a distant row lands on an estimate, so an identical command is re-issued once the geometry has moved and the reveal is still incomplete (at most 3 times per target). Both bounds are checked window-free. A lazy grid's scroll to a row or section header it hasn't laid out lands on an estimate and repeats it, so a reveal whose target frame has been measured scrolls the ScrollPosition to that exact content offset; only a never-measured tile falls back to ScrollViewReader. With that, the native scene audit, demo window and self-test (1,574 checks) all pass, every gallery interaction set included (full 12/12; 1280, 300 and 560 8/8 each). Hosted CI then exposed a harness race: settle() accepted a row after one synchronous layout pass, because cached frames and the model's style are right before SwiftUI delivers the change, so the user-scroll cancel could precede the gallery consuming the Undo's request; that later request legitimately re-armed the reveal. settle() now also requires the reveal to have consumed the live request (selection, viewport and Fine Tune), the unattached host's measured viewport to match the gallery's real frame, and the state to hold across one more turn; the assertions are unchanged and their names carry the snapshot values. The mac-ui suite runs inside HeavyMediaTests so its app-process audits don't starve the timed heavy media tests on the three-core runner. The self-test's process deadline is 360 s (its 1,568 checks finished at about 240 s); the sidebar-overlay check polls for the overlay instead of sleeping a fixed 250 ms; and the UI harness prints failing check names and the app log's tail on a nonzero exit, because hosted CI keeps no UI evidence.

### 2026-10-29 — sol-mac-voicelab — Publish English τ

- Supersedes the W2 withheld outcome below. The lead-approved rule pools clean impostors from dev-clean and test-clean at both input rates, takes P99.9 by linear interpolation at `(n-1)*0.999`, adds `max(0.002, measured cross-runtime drift)` and rounds up to 0.01 with decimal ceiling. The candidate is frozen before disjoint validation; every corpus × rate must independently retain FAR ≤ 0.5% and same-speaker pass ≥ 95%. Tool, schema, fixture-drift check and production catalogue lookup validate the new policy, percentile and rounding evidence rather than a maximum-impostor bound. Python and Swift tests cover exact decimal boundaries without epsilon relaxation.

All eight conditions pass: dev-clean at each rate has 5/7,020 false accepts and 120/120 own passes; test-clean 8/7,020 and 120/120; dev-other 0/4,752 and 99/99; test-other 2/4,752 and 98/99. Worst FAR is 0.113960%, worst same-speaker pass 98.989899%. There are 80 clean calibration plus 66 disjoint harder speakers, 438 distinct source utterances, 876 rate renders, 47,088 impostor and 876 same-speaker pairs. Pooled results are 30/47,088 and 874/876; these pairs/renders are correlated, not independent trials. The catalogue records the date, rule, corpus and counts with `outcome: passed`; calibration curves and hashes are regenerated. Pair scores and all eight real-ONNX parity fixtures regenerate byte-identically.

### 2026-10-27 — Source extraction follows the stream tick (sol-followups-8)

- Voice Lab §7.3 now defines the both-platform extraction rule: `abs(t * rate - next) < max(1, rate / timestampTimescale)`, strictly, places a decoded buffer at `next`; otherwise half-up placement refuses a gap and skips overlap. Decoded counts advance the grid, so timestamp drift cannot accumulate past one tick. Sub-tick container gaps cannot be detected.
- Mac uses `CMTime.value * rate / timescale` in Int64 when the multiplication does not overflow and the division is exact. For fractional results it divides the nonoverflowing integer product in binary64; for an overflowing product it multiplies and divides in binary64. It never first converts the timestamp to seconds. The same helper owns strict continuity and the gap refusal used by extraction.
- `VoiceLabSourceAudioTests` passes 14 tests (17 argument cases), including every sample across five generated LPCM writer buffers, submitted at 48,000 and 1,000 timescales; exact one-sample and one-/two-coarse-tick gap refusals on constructed PCM buffers; ten coarse timestamp placements following decoded counts; and a constructed buffer 0.6 samples early with no skipped sample. Existing half-open range, downmix, resampling, limits and checked-file tests pass. The focused suite takes 1.182 s on this shared host.
- Writer limitation, not a claimed coarse-file integration test: `AVAssetWriterInput` raises `NSInvalidArgumentException` for a nondefault audio `mediaTimeScale`. Supplying 1,000-scale presentation timestamps is accepted, but the resulting LPCM MOV track has timescale 48,000. The writer also flattens the submitted one-sample and 96-sample gaps into exact contiguous PCM; `writerNormalizesLPCMTimestampGaps` checks every resulting sample. Therefore the strict gap, coarse tick and fractional-early shapes are tested directly on the production placement helper with constructed `CMReadySampleBuffer` timestamps, not misrepresented as persisted writer gaps. No binaries are committed.

### 2026-10-27 — sol-vl-word-diff round 2: current API and verification

This entry supersedes the historical round-1 API and figures below. Decisions 1–8 are implemented in order, one commit each; `origin/main` `5d6a295a5b0c4a9c63701a3f72255cd487d1dcc6` is merged, with all entries from both contract histories retained and track entries first.

- **Whole timed items (§8.2 steps 3–6).** Raw `ops` remain token-level, but actionable hunks widen to entire source items before classification, retain kept surface tokens positionally, and coalesce on shared items or touching regions. Partial deletion/replacement is `voice`, not an item cut. New hunk field `correctedTexts: [String]` is empty except for `textOnly`, where it contains one complete corrected text per `sourceWordIds`, positionally joining each item's new tokens with single spaces. Source indices still address word-only tokens, now at whole-item boundaries. Insert-only anchors are unchanged. The two-equal-word merge threshold counts distinct source word items, not tokens. Six shared whole-item/bridge regressions cover partial cut, partial replace, interior insertion, partial case correction, two edits in one item, and a three-token bridge item.
- **Nonwords (§8.2 step 6; §8.4 step 4).** Retain events and unlabeled speech in source order as voice-merge barriers, regardless of count or time; insert-only hunks use their anchor position. New hunk field `absorbedItemIds: [String]` lists strictly interior nonword IDs in source order when a single user-edit voice hunk straddles them. Accepting that hunk replaces those items; the App must show each as removed in the read-only diff line. Cut/textOnly hunks affect only word IDs and have empty absorbed IDs. Ten shared vectors cover both barrier kinds, insertion anchors, one equal word, a 0.1 s gap, both absorption kinds, cut/correction preservation, and exclusion of edge nonwords. No App or design.md edit is made here; the lead adds the matching design sentence at merge.
- **Header pricing (§8.2; §8.4 step 4).** `VoiceLabDiffSummary.make(..., patchTexts: [String?]? = nil)`. Supply one optional complete patch text per voice hunk in order, including left window bridge + new words + right window bridge. A missing entry or omitted list returns nil/null cost and omits the amount suffix; never price hunk tokens as fallback. A supplied count different from the voice-hunk count throws `VoiceLabLengthBudgetError.invalidInput`. Only a fully populated list can price its total Unicode scalars, with no separator between hunks. Shared vectors distinguish 3-scalar hunk `new` from 14-scalar complete `left new right`, all-missing text, and one missing text among two windows; the existing focused test pins mismatched-count refusal.
- **Fit quote (§8.9.1).** `VoiceLabFitMeter.measure(..., rate: Double, newWords: String, multipleHunks: Bool = false)`. `newWords` is required even for one hunk: the hunk's `newTokens` joined with single spaces (`hunk.newTokens.joined(separator: " ")`). They include the diff-merge bridge words a merged hunk re-voices (§8.2 step 6) and never the window-search bridges (§8.4 step 4). Duration still uses complete patch `text`; multi-hunk quotes always use `newWords`, never that complete text. `multi-hunk-quote-excludes-bridges` and `multi-hunk-quote-includes-merge-bridges` pin the distinction. Candidate provenance/key/seconds/fallback are documented in the following round-2 entry.
- **Current shared fixtures.** `word-diff-cases.json`: **129 cases, 261,536 bytes**, SHA-256 `cacfa995d303667bb4b84ac7ffba38378769d8cc2a05a639c7c90f25a00e6972`. `length-budget-cases.json`: **53 cases, 46,301 bytes**, SHA-256 `8819ee96ec3501cc192d3325c52d5b811a5ac5a8188230dc3dfc516d8befe371`. The lead's merge added `multi-hunk-quote-includes-merge-bridges` and made `anchor-after-event` a true insert-only hunk anchored after an event (anchor 0.5 s); the former replace case is now `replace-before-event`. Only the three token-limit vectors use declarative repetitions: `sourceRepeat` (`token`, `count`, `idPrefix`, `timingStep`; item `i` is `idPrefix + i` over [`i × timingStep`, start + `timingStep / 2`]), `newTextRepeat` (`count` copies of `token` joined by single spaces) and `expected.repeatedOps` (ordered op kind, source/new tokens, ID prefix and count), as shared/fixtures/voice-lab/README.md specifies. ### 2026-10-27 — sol-vl-word-diff round 1: historical Rewrite consumer API

All additions are pure in `Sources/VoxClipCore/Domain/VoiceLab/`, owned by `sol-vl-word-diff`, with identical Windows type/field names under platform casing. No App, persistence, dependencies or provider calls change.

- **`VoiceLabWordDiff.swift`.** `VoiceLabWordDiff.diff(source: [TimedWord], newText: String) throws -> VoiceLabWordDiffResult`. Pass selected source items. Only `word` items participate; existing whitespace/NFC/matching helpers preserve display surfaces. IDs must be nonempty/unique, word text nonblank, starts ordered/nonnegative, and times finite with `end >= start`. `VoiceLabWordDiffError` is `.invalidInput` or `.tokenLimit(side: VoiceLabDiffSide, count: Int, limit: Int)`; sides are `source | newText`, limit is 2,000 **tokens per side**, checked before DP allocation.
- **Diff output.** `VoiceLabWordDiffResult {ops: [VoiceLabDiffOp], hunks: [VoiceLabDiffHunk]}`. Op fields: `kind: VoiceLabDiffOpKind` (`equal | delete | insert`), `sourceWordId`, `sourceToken`, `newToken` (`String?`); inserts lack source fields, deletes lack new tokens. Hunk fields: `kind: VoiceLabDiffHunkKind` (`cut | textOnly | voice`), `sourceWordIds`, `newTokens`, `bridgeWordIds` (`[String]`), `sourceStartIndex`, `sourceEndIndex` (`Int`), `anchorSeconds: Double?`. IDs are distinct in source order. Half-open indices address word-only **tokens**, not transcript items. Inserts anchor at the next token's start, or final token's end, or 0. §8.2 pins the forward suffix-DP delete-on-tie backtrack, stable deletes-before-inserts per replace region, and merge across only equal ops when <=2 words OR end-to-start time <=0.35 s. Bridges, including corrected surfaces, become spoken tokens; absorbed textOnly hunks are not duplicated.
- **`VoiceLabPatchBudget.swift`.** `VoiceLabPatchBudget.evaluate(diff: VoiceLabWordDiffResult, transcript: [TimedWord]) -> VoiceLabPatchBudgetResult {voiceTokenCount: Int, wordItems: Int, flag: VoiceLabPatchBudgetFlag, copy: String?}`. Pass the **whole transcript**. Count merged voice-hunk new tokens, bridges included, against only word-kind items. `flag` is `reRecordInstead` iff integer `count * 100 > 15 * wordItems || count > 60`, otherwise `allowed`. `disabledCopy` is exactly "That's a lot to re-voice. Re-record these sentences instead."; allowed copy is nil. This disables neither cuts nor corrections.
- **`VoiceLabLengthBudget.swift`: rate.** `VoiceLabLengthBudget.rate(transcript: [TimedWord], pauseThresholdSeconds: Double = 1, candidates: [VoiceLabRateCandidate] = []) throws -> VoiceLabSpeechRate`. Supply the take's pause threshold and candidates for **one** selected (voice, model, language) key. Construct `VoiceLabRateCandidate(text: String, firstCharStart: Double, lastCharEnd: Double)`. Nonempty candidates override transcript evidence; otherwise existing pause detection on all items and nonword barriers define voiced word spans. `VoiceLabSpeechRate` holds `charactersPerSecond: Double`, `source: VoiceLabSpeechRateSource` (`transcript | candidates`), `textLength: Int`, `voicedSeconds: Double`. Length means NFC letters/digits. `VoiceLabLengthBudgetError` is `.invalidInput` for malformed input/overflow or `.rateUnavailable` for no positive evidence, never a silent fallback from bad candidates.
- **Length ranges.** `estimate(text: String, rate: Double) throws -> Double`; `calculate(window: VoiceLabWindow, incomingSilence: Double?, outgoingSilence: Double?, bridgeLength: Int, rate: Double) throws -> VoiceLabLengthRange`; `passage(window: VoiceLabWindow, transcript: [TimedWord], incomingSilence: Double?, outgoingSilence: Double?, rate: Double) throws -> VoiceLabLengthRange`; `fullTake(transcript: [TimedWord], rate: Double) throws -> VoiceLabLengthRange`. Result fields: `targetSeconds`, `edgePadSeconds` (`Double`), `bridgeLength`, `minChars`, `maxChars` (`Int`), `advisory: Bool`. Feed `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.lendableSilence`'s pair directly: hard=nil, gap/none=finite nonnegative. Place the **whole selection as one replace hunk** for `passage`, also for a Fix-with-AI line; it resolves bridge IDs through `VoiceLabTextLength`. `fullTake` is advisory, first-word start through last-word end, `e=f=0`. §8.9.1 fixes binary64 order and default-trim edge padding. Results fit Int32; rewrite services still enforce request limits (600 s / 5,000 chars), not this calculator.
- **`VoiceLabFitMeter.swift`.** `VoiceLabFitMeter.measure(text: String, window: VoiceLabWindow, incomingSilence: Double?, outgoingSilence: Double?, rate: Double, multipleHunks: Bool = false, newWords: String? = nil) throws -> VoiceLabFitMeterResult`. Text is the **complete patch**, including window-search and diff-merge bridges. Fields: `estimateSeconds`, `gapSeconds (=w)`, `lowerBoundSeconds`, `upperBoundSeconds` (`Double`), `verdict: VoiceLabFitVerdict` (`fits | willChangeLength`), `copy: String`. Bounds inclusive: subtract hard=0.06 or gap/none=0.3 pads below; borrow `max(0, outsideSilence-0.15)` only at gap seams above. None seams never borrow. Copy uses `toFixed1`; multiple hunks add `“new words” · `, taking the quoted words from optional `newWords` (default: `text`), so window-search bridges need not appear in the quote. §8.9.1 now explicitly follows §8.9.2's protected-silence borrowing; the paid-take planner is unchanged.
- **`VoiceLabDiffSummary.swift`.** `VoiceLabDiffSummary.make(diff: VoiceLabWordDiffResult, meters: [VoiceLabFitMeterResult], catalog: VoiceLabPriceCatalog? = nil, model: String? = nil, candidates: Int = 1, characterMultiplier: Double? = nil, patchTexts: [String]? = nil) throws -> VoiceLabDiffSummaryResult`. Supply exactly one meter per merged voice hunk in order. Fields: `cutCount`, `revoicedPhrases` (`Int`), `estimateSeconds: Double`, `costEstimate: CostEstimate?`, `copy: String`. Cuts count distinct cut IDs; duration sums each hunk once, not each candidate. Optional `patchTexts` supplies one complete spoken patch per voice hunk, including window-search bridges; otherwise use each hunk's space-joined new tokens. Bill Unicode scalars (no inter-hunk separator), not letters/digits; mismatched patch-text counts throw invalidInput. Existing pricing/presentation determines amount copy. No catalog/no voices yields nil cost; unknown model yields unknown-price cost and no amount. Singular/plural header copy is pure and implies no approval/spend.

These five files and all result/error/enum declarations named above extend the public type inventory. The round-1 verification entry records vector counts, hashes and tests.

### 2026-10-27 — sol-win-redesign round 3 — Relink tolerance boundary

The lead-approved A4 rule compares rounded millisecond grid values: `abs(jsRound(probedSeconds × 1000) − jsRound(storedSeconds × 1000)) ≤ 50`. This changes the Mac only at the relink tolerance boundary; 10.00 versus 10.05 or 9.95 is accepted, while 10.051 or 9.949 is refused. The existing 0.25 versus 0.3001 vector is now accepted after grid rounding. The 12-case fixture is generated by `tools/fixtures/projects/generate_relink.py`, documented beside it and replayed by both Cores. Identity, explicit confirmation, safe writing and transcript/edit retention are unchanged.

- Lead fixes at merge: `RelinkDecision.decide` compares content keys as UTF-8 bytes (`key.utf8.elementsEqual(storedKey.utf8)`), matching the C# ordinal comparison, so canonically equivalent but byte-different keys need confirmation on both platforms. The generator now rounds as both Cores do (binary64 × 1000, then jsRound) and adds `binary64-half-millisecond`.
- Mac follow-up done in round-3 B1 (design §5.4.4, editing §10.3 step 4): PreviewTimeline uses the missing-media caption-position exception even with `previewError`; moving, undoing and saving remain available over the black stage. Native UI coverage and light/dark captures supplement the model test; actual pointer dragging remains a real-window check.

### 2026-10-27 — Pre-merge fixes (opus-mac-redesign round 1e)

- **The title follows the document's name (lead decision 1).** When Save As, Move or Rename gives the file a new base name, `Project.title` takes that name in the same write, but only while the title is unset, equals the video's base name as import sets it (trimmed, at most 200 scalars) or equals the previous file's base name. Any other title stays. `VoxClipDocument.followingTitle(_:previousNames:destination:)` holds the rule.
  - Save As writes the new title in its snapshot and adopts it when the save succeeds.
  - A cross-folder Move writes it into the copy and adopts it only after the original is removed.
  - A same-folder Rename keeps AppKit's own move. It then writes the title in place with an autosave-in-place save, which adds no undo step and no edited dot. The move calls back while it still holds the document's file access, so that save starts on the next main-actor turn and takes its own. Started inside the callback, it outlived the move's access and AppKit stopped the app with "invoked outside of file access" (the first save-boundary run).
  - `EditorModel.replace` carries the current title into every edit. Undoing an edit made before a Save As or Rename therefore keeps the new title.
  - File > Duplicate treats the original's file name as a previous name, so the copy's first Save As retitles it too.
  - One display rule everywhere: `Project.title`, else the file's base name, else the video's base name.
    - The window title and sidebar header read it through the shell, and the shell bumps an observed title revision when the title follows the name.
    - Home cards, Home search and Recent rows read it through `RecentProjects`, which rereads files that changed.
    - File > Open Recent builds its titles with `AppModel.recentTitle(_:)`: the open project's title, else the title `RecentProjects` read, else the file name.
  - design §2 "Window title" gains one sentence.
  - The save-boundary audit has main's checks back, "first Save As updates the native title" and "native Rename updates the title", now with exact titles and the title read back from the file. It adds:
    - the second Save As;
    - Rename adds no undo step and no edited dot;
    - after Rename the window, the sidebar header, the Home card and Open Recent all read the new name;
    - Save As "A" then Rename "B" shows "B" everywhere, and an undo after the Save As keeps "A";
    - a title that isn't a default stays through Rename.
  - The audit's Home card wait allows 10 s. The card is read at utility priority and draws two caption cards, which a busy Mac took over 3 s to do.
  - The audit's wait after Revert now starts after the editor's reload. It used to pass on the editor's state from before the revert, so the next step began while the reload was still checking the media.
  - Not covered: a rename in Finder, or Revert To a version from before a rename, shows the stored title until the next Save As, Move or Rename.
- **Crash recovery of never-saved work (lead decision 2).** Outside harness sessions the main window is restorable, with `restorationClass = MainWindowController` and identifier `voxclip.main`.
  - Its restorable state records only an untitled project's `autosavedContentsFileURL`, as a secure-coded `NSURL`. A saved project records nothing, so a relaunch shows Home and saved work reopens from Recent.
  - On restore, `WorkspaceShell.reopenAutosave(_:)` reopens the autosave with `reopenDocument(for: nil, withContentsOf:display: false)` and attaches it through `replaceProject`. The project stays untitled and edited, so a normal quit still shows the keep/delete prompt.
  - AppKit's document recovery and the restorable state can both reach one autosave. `reopenAutosave` returns early when the open project already is that autosave, and `WorkspaceShell.closesRecoveredCopy(_:)` closes a second copy, from either path, without touching the file. AppKit's own reopen of an autosave that is already open returns the open document.
  - `addWindowController` makes `NSDocumentController` the restoration class, so attach and detach set `MainWindowController` again. Attach, detach and every save or autosave invalidate the state.
  - Versions windows aren't restorable, and `applicationSupportsSecureRestorableState` returns true. Harness sessions keep the window non-restorable, so test runs leave no state behind.
  - design §2 "Launch" gains one sentence.
  - Checked by hand with the screen unlocked, on a copy of a bundle.sh build with its own bundle ID: import, three cuts, AppKit's own autosave, kill -9, relaunch.
    - Seven runs, relaunched directly and through LaunchServices, reopened the same project in the main window: untitled, edited, with its cuts and the movie's name, the only window and document.
    - In the runs that went on to quit, the keep/delete panel showed and Cancel kept the project. One quit, with "Close windows when quitting an app" off, hung in the probe itself, which quit from a task waiting on the settings flush.
  - On this Mac (macOS 27) the reopen comes from AppKit's document recovery, which calls `makeWindowControllers` on the reopened autosave.
    - `restoreWindow` was never called, even with the window's state written before the kill and with "Close windows when quitting an app" off.
    - A non-restorable window recovered the same way.
    - The restorable window stays as decided.
  - Rerun on the final code with the duplicate guard, once relaunched directly and once through LaunchServices: all nine checks passed both times, including the prompt's name.
  - `VoxClipDocument.defaultDraftName()` names an untitled project by its title, else its video's name, never "Untitled" (design §2). The keep/delete prompt names the project by `displayName`, and its Save As field read "probe-take.voxclip" in the kill -9 runs. AppKit still names the autosave file itself "Unsaved VoxClip Document".
- **A rename being typed (3).** The inline rename lives in the shell (`renaming`, `renameDraft`). `leave()` keeps the typed title before edits freeze, which covers ⌘W, the close button, Finder and Dock opens, drops and replace. Quit keeps it before the documents are reviewed. Escape still keeps the old title.
- **Model fallback note (4).** After a run the fallback model answered, `EditorModel.fallbackNote` ("Answered by {fallback} because {model} was busy.", ai.md §4.3) shows under the Suggested header in 11 pt `text.secondary` while suggestions remain. It is announced once, in the run's announcement: "Found 3 clips. Answered by …". The unused `clipAnswer` is gone.
- **Find Clips focus (5).** The "Find clips" step no longer carries `.setActiveClip`. The first new suggestion in transcript order becomes `EditorModel.focusedSuggestion`: the sidebar list scrolls to it, takes keyboard focus and draws the keyboard focus ring on that row. The scope, playhead, Export and subtitle stay. Return keeps the focused suggestion and ⌫ dismisses it, and focus moves to the next suggestion. ↑ or ↓ selects the focused suggestion, and the list carries on from there. Any selection change clears the focus.
- **Find Clips below 960 (6).** Below 960 the sidebar shows as an overlay over the detail while the column stays collapsed (`SidebarOverlay`: 252 wide, `bg.sunken`, a trailing hairline and a shadow).
  - `revealSidebar()` (the Find Clips callout and progress), the toolbar's sidebar button and View > Show Sidebar show it.
  - `fitColumns()` leaves it alone below 960 and drops it at 960 and wider, where the column returns.
  - Escape or a click on a row hides it. The arrow keys keep it open.
- **Export Clip… gate (7).** The self-test sets up an earlier export of another clip, with its result and an error. It then right-clicks the clip row in the sidebar list (`NSTableView.menu(for:)` in an offscreen window) and performs the menu's own "Export Clip…" item with `performActionForItem(at:)`.
  - AppKit runs no menu action while an app-modal session is open, so the check fails if one is.
  - One was open in some runs. `DebugRegressions.media` left an edited document in a folder it then removed, and AppKit's "could not be autosaved" alert stayed up for the rest of the run. That check now closes its documents.
  - `DebugPersistence` was a second source: it left an edited document at `roundtrip.voxclip`, a file it never wrote, so AppKit's autosave failed whenever it next ran. It now closes its document too. The check prints the alert's text if one is ever open.
  - With no alert up, AppKit's autosave ran in harness sessions and left the self-test's never-saved projects in the user's `~/Library/Autosave Information`. In DEBUG harness sessions, `VoxClipDocument.autosave(withImplicitCancellability:completionHandler:)` now skips a never-saved project with no autosave location. One that a check gave a location still autosaves there.
  - With the row's call reverted to `showExport = true`, the self-test fails only this check, 274 of 275 passing ("Export shown true for clip 1").
- **Accent in a bundled build (8).** Measured from a bundle.sh build with the screen unlocked, in a real window, as sRGB. AppKit's `controlAccentColor` is Petrol: #1C6B69 light, #237D7A dark.
  - Focused list: the selection is the system's emphasized fill of that accent, #437B7B light and #4E8D8C dark. The white label measures 4.82:1 and 3.81:1.
  - Key window, list not focused: a grey fill (#DDDDDB light, #50555B dark), with the Home glyph in the accent (#2E8785 light, #5DB7B9 dark).
  - Inactive window: the system's grey unemphasized state, fill #EBEAE9 with glyph #CBCBC9 light, and #3D4448 with #767B7E dark.
  - No accent is passed explicitly. A DEBUG build run without its bundle has no asset catalog and draws the system accent: #418FF8 in the scene audit.
- **Merge (9).** Main at 196b25c7 is merged as one merge commit. AppModel keeps `SharePostText.preflight()` without `registerFonts()`, and CONTRACTS keeps both sides, newest first.
- **Hosted CI.** The long-expression check (`-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300`) flagged the bodies of `SidebarList` and `WorkspaceView`, which this round extended. The list's key handling (`keys(_:)`), the split view (`split`), the sheets (`sheets(on:)`), the detail column and the pause-breath change (`applyPauseBreath()`) are now their own expressions, in the same modifier order. Later runs flagged only single member accesses at 300 to 330 ms, a different set each run, and the last run flagged nothing.
- **Probes.** `DebugProbes` adds `-recoveryProbe prepare|verify -probeFolder … [-probeMovie …]` and `-accentProbe <folder>` to DEBUG builds. They are run by hand on a copy of a bundle.sh build that has its own bundle ID. They launch like a user's app, skip update checks, and pass every path as a flag value, because AppKit opens a loose path argument as a document.
- **Media check during a save.** `EditorModel.resolveMediaIdentity()` treated any change to `media.source` during its check as a cancellation, including the relative path a Save As or Move writes.
  - A Save As that landed while a reload checked the media left `mediaResolved` false, and edits and undo were refused until the project reopened. Main has the same guard.
  - The check now goes stale only when the content key, size or absolute path changes.
  - The self-test rewrites the relative path while a check waits on the project store. With main's guard it fails ("checked false, resolved false"), 276 of 277 passing.

### 2026-10-27 — CLI stop-path timing and early cancellation (sol-flake-detime, round 2)

- Supersedes round 1 on base `196b25c7`; merged `origin/main` `557cdefc`. `VOXCLIP_TEST_INTERRUPT_TIMING=1` records monotonic uptime nanoseconds at exporter entry, the first SIGINT dispatch-handler invocation and immediately before exit 6 after cleanup and cancellation output. It emits exactly one stderr line, `VOXCLIP_TEST_INTERRUPT_TIMING handler=<uptime-nanoseconds> exportStart=<uptime-nanoseconds> exit=<uptime-nanoseconds>`. `exportStart=0` means exporter entry was prevented. Other values or an unset variable disable instrumentation. Architecture §5.6 and §9.4 document the mode; the JSON error schema is unchanged.
- The stress test keeps 30 concurrent pairs and seed `0x564f58434c495034`. Each child waits up to 60 s for `<output>.partial` before applying the seeded 0–2 s offset. It asserts exporter entry before the handler and `exit − max(handler, exportStart) ≤ 2 s`, exit 6, silent `cancelled`, all cleanup assertions and preservation of the unrelated partial-like file. The 15 s hang guard starts when SIGINT is sent; parent-observed latency is diagnostic. Sendable reference types hold lock-protected callback state. The no-instrumentation regression has a five-minute limit, including the 120 s fixture-generation budget, and still requires empty quiet stderr.
- Production cancellation checkpoints now separate share preflight, project/source loading, probe, transcript decode, contentKey, planning, Voice Lab preflight, captions and exporter setup, including optional edited-audio staging. Cancellation is observed after the current preparation step returns. `VOXCLIP_TEST_PRE_EXPORT_PAUSE=1` requires the timing mode; it pauses after source import and before planning for at most 60 s, emits a readiness line and returns on cancellation to the production checkpoint. The early-interrupt regression sends SIGINT 0.05 s after readiness and asserts exit 6, `exportStart=0`, no final or partial file and a 15 s hang guard, without a tight timing bound.
- Caption cancellation uses distinct first/last shadow indices: neon has two selected indices and editorial has one. Across three widths and two cache budgets, each run makes 18 distinct cancellations and 18 byte-for-byte recovery comparisons. Each hook requests cancellation after a shadow-mask mutation; all attempts observe it at the existing check after the shadow block, and no interrupted band is published. The recovery comparisons show that a cancelled band is never published and that the next render is byte-identical; they cannot show renderer-state poisoning, because `CaptionRenderPool` discards a renderer whose render was cancelled and each attempt starts a fresh pool. Cold/reused controls and deterministic shadow-failure coverage remain.
- Verification on the merged tree, macOS 27.0.1 / Swift 6.4, with the absolute worktree `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, live providers and downloads off: `swift build --jobs 2` passed; Core passed 545 reported tests in 38 suites (8 expected skips); CI's full Media command, `swift test --jobs 2 --filter VoxClipMediaTests --skip CaptionParityTests`, passed 258 in 27 suites (12 expected test skips, plus 2 XCTest skips); CaptionParity passed all 4 tests, including 262 samples and 38 tiles. The full Media stress run peaked at 0.147665 s internally and 0.154938 s in the parent. The long-expression test build passed with no warnings in the four changed Swift files; only unchanged files were flagged. The exact CI secret pattern was clean over all surviving changed files.
- Five repetitions of `swift test --skip-build --jobs 2 --filter 'CLIInterruptTests|CaptionCancellationTests|builderAndEditedWaveUseMixedSourceGrid'` passed 35/35 selected tests, 300 stress exports, 5 early interrupts, 5 no-instrumentation cancellations and 90 caption cancellations across 18 distinct cases per run, with no watchdog firing. Internal stop-path maxima, in run order: 0.502357, 0.259565, 0.532358, 0.142965, 0.271832 s. Early-interrupt parent-observed exit times: 0.013449, 0.012973, 0.022811, 0.010230, 0.008019 s; every early record had `exportStart=0`. Parent-observed stress latency peaked at 0.575054 s. The host was shared and busy (recorded one-minute load averages about 19–69), with no artificial load. The Voice Lab builder passed 5/5 in 5.633, 4.502, 4.196, 3.311 and 2.389 s; its earlier timeout remains unexplained and `VoiceLabCompositionTests.swift` is unchanged. Hosted Xcode 26 confirmation remains pending. Windows needs no change: its CLI passes the Ctrl+C cancellation token to every pre-export step, checks it before the exporter creates a partial, deletes owned partials and exits 6 (`CliOutputParityTests` pins a pre-cancelled export); it has no wall-clock subprocess timing test.

### 2026-10-27 — Rewrite budget round-four parity and compatibility

- Lead decisions in the round-four rewrite-budget brief: both Cores normalize transcript word text and corrections in memory before project-load text validation (editing §10.1). Older LV+T project text opens without a prompt or warning; the next save writes NFC. Word ids and source timings never change. `projects/normalization/legacy-nfc.voxclip` and its expected model pin the result.

Seven shared context-resolution vectors include a 1,500-word cloud send and its local refusal, replayed by both rewrite services.
- Claude Code chooses the greatest output-token usage (`outputTokens`, else `output_tokens`, else zero), with the smallest UTF-8 ordinal key on ties. A malformed Claude envelope or malformed required envelope field returns `cliFailed` with the first stderr line, without marker classification. Both strict parsers reject number tokens over 1,024 bytes (ai §4.5, §6.8, §12.8). The 35 shared CLI vectors cover those rules and Codex exec/app-server LF-only splitting with CRLF, trailing CR, U+2028 and BOM.
- Merged origin/main 557cdefc, preserving the Windows redesign, caption gallery Core and both contract histories. No App/UI consumer was changed by this round; App/UI changes in the merge belong to main.
- `EditingPrimitives.nfc` splits at all four inert jamo U+1113, U+1176, U+11A7 and U+11C3 and uses Foundation's ICU-backed NFC transform, avoiding NSString's Hangul bounds and Unicode 16 recomposition defects. The 39 text-length vectors and `ingest/clean-words-unicode` pin the added boundaries; project tests also pin Kirat Rai and Todhri composition.
