### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab fit planner (opus-vl-fit-planner)

- Added the pure §8.9.2 silence-only duration fit in `Domain/VoiceLab/VoiceLabFitPlanner.swift`: `VoiceLabFitPlanner.plan(window:incomingSilence:outgoingSilence:take:tokens:sourceSampleRate:) throws -> VoiceLabFitResult`. The window is the placed `VoiceLabWindow` with its seam kinds. The outside silence of a `gap` or `none` seam is in seconds, and a `hard` seam passes nil. A `gap` seam's silence is its lendable silence: the kept source contiguous with it on its outer side, as §8.4 step 5 measures it for the final window, up to the nearest kept item outside the window. A `VoiceLabFitTake` gives the paid asset's frame count and rate, the trims in asset seconds, the aligned words and the asset's 10 ms envelope.
- The result is `.exact(VoiceLabFitPlan)` or `.changedDuration(VoiceLabFitPlan, naturalOut:, residualSeconds:)`. A plan holds:
  - the edits: `VoiceLabFitEdit` values, `remove` or `insert`, with start and count in asset frames;
  - one `VoiceLabFitSeam` per side, with its pad frames and borrowed source samples;
  - the final trim frames, and the window after borrowing in seconds and samples;
  - the output frames, and the word times relative to the output start, all `synthetic: true`.

  No caller uses the planner yet: the fitted WAV (§8.9.3) and the candidate builder belong to later tracks.
- `VoiceLabPatchConstants` gains the §8.3 fit constants: `SEAM_PAD_MAX` 0.3, `HARD_SEAM_PAD_MAX` 0.060, `PAUSE_FLOOR_PHRASE` 0.060, `PAUSE_FLOOR_PUNCT` 0.150, `PAUSE_GROWTH_MAX` 0.25, `LENGTH_AIM_MIN` 0.92 and `LENGTH_AIM_MAX` 0.97. The existing constants are unchanged.
- `VoiceLabGeometryError` gains `fitTouchesVoice`. The planner checks every plan before it returns it:
  - both pads are non-negative;
  - the edits are in order and apart, inside the trims;
  - no edit or `SEAM_FADE` join overlaps a word span or an envelope frame at or above `VOICED_FRAME`;
  - the frames add up to the output.

  A failure is a planner fault, never a property of the take. Invalid input throws `invalidInput()`, `alignmentUnavailable`, `alignmentMismatch` or `invalidWordTiming`, checked in that order, so the planner never fails silently and never traps. Assets must have fewer than 2^31 frames, the source rate must lie in 1 Hz to 1 MHz, and `trimEnd` must come after `trimStart`.
- Every ambiguity in §8.9.2 is resolved by its most literal reading, written into voice-lab.md §8.9.2 under "Pinned readings" with the vectors that pin it.
- `VoiceLabRenderCapabilityError.pitchPreservingFitUnverified` is renamed `nonunityFitUnsupported`. It is now a permanent refusal (ADR-R30, voice-lab.md §10.5) with the message "This Voice Lab take was stretched to fit its window, which VoxClip does not support. The generated audio and edits are unchanged." The refusal and where it is thrown are unchanged. The message keeps the word "stretched", which the App's debug recipe check and `VoiceLabCallerTests` look for. Callers outside Media's preflight and its tests match the type, not the case.
- Review round 2 (lead decisions; voice-lab.md §8.9.2):
  - **Residual.** When no pad or trim can take a negative rounding residual, the seams borrow the fewest more source samples whose widened window holds the planned frames, within what they can lend and split again, and the residual, now zero or more, pads the gap seam that borrowed, incoming first. A valid take is no longer refused with `invalidWordTiming` for its residual; a residual still negative throws `fitTouchesVoice`. The pad, tail trim, lead trim order is unchanged everywhere else.
  - **Word times.** Each word's start and end are clamped in binary64 to `windowEnd − windowStart` for `.exact` and to that plus `residualSeconds` for `.changedDuration`, the recipe's `outDuration`, so every word meets the §10.5 word rule.
  - **Lendable silence.** New public `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.lendableSilence(project:scope:window:) throws -> (incoming: Double?, outgoing: Double?)` returns each gap seam's lendable silence in seconds, and nil for a `hard` seam, and transcript silence to the nearest window word for `none`. It stops at a kept item, a cut item's reach, a cut pause, a clip edge or another replacement's outer fade half. `place` and the helper now share private `Take` and `ScopeSource` types holding `place`'s own take checks and §8.4 scope setup, so `place`'s results and error order are unchanged. The internal `VoiceLabGeometry.check` takes an optional envelope.
  - **Media scratch root.** `CompositionBuilder` has an internal `init(scratchRoot:)`, and the internal `AVExporter(capacityProvider:scratchRoot:appVersion:)` passes the root to its builder and its capacity estimates. `VoiceLabAudioRenderer.stage` and internal `VoiceLabAssets.prepare` overloads put the asset snapshots and the mix under that root. Every public entry point keeps the temporary directory. `nonunityFitRefusesBeforeSnapshotAndPublication` and `combinedOutputPreflightCountsWAVBeforeAnyStageAndSeparatesVolumes` give each case its own scratch folder and require it to stay empty, instead of diffing the shared temporary directory, which other suites and processes write to.
- `VoiceLabFitPlannerTests` replays all 2,112 shared `fit-cases.json` vectors (schema `voxclip-voice-lab-fit-cases/1`, sha256 `cd111d76ceeaa111709dde3b2bd6ebae33cb73f325d598bd5c12f65fe51520cb`). `tools/fixtures/voice-lab/generate.py` writes them through the standard-library reference `generate_fit.py`, and `fit-README.md` describes them. There are 112 named cases in 11 categories, and 2,000 property cases from LCG seed 20261027: 1,646 `exact`, 299 `changedDuration` and 55 typed refusals. Round 2 changed the expected output of `residual-unplaced` and `property-0985`, both `invalidWordTiming` before and `exact` now, and added `residual-raise-outgoing-borrow`, `round-word-end-clamped`, `refuse-trim-end-before-start`, `refuse-source-rate-too-low` and `refuse-source-rate-too-high`. Each plan is checked again on this Core for:
  - the voice invariant;
  - the centring and limits of each edit;
  - the seam limits;
  - the word and window times;
  - the full §10.5 word rule: both end bounds, positive seconds and source-sample spans, and starts at or after the previous end;
  - the `exact` output length, `jsRound(n × assetRate / sourceRate)` frames.

  Non-finite inputs, which JSON cannot carry, are tested directly.
- `VoiceLabWindowTests` reads `window-cases.json` schema `voxclip-voice-lab-window-cases/4` (606 cases, sha256 `21687da68e24e563263c3a9370776a3aed10e2e484ff430f19e37db011178631`). The new `lendableSilence` category places one window for each stop and compares the helper's result exactly. `lendableSilenceFuzz` records that every window the reference's two fuzzes accept still renders when each gap seam lends all it can beyond `PAUSE_SLACK_KEEP`.
- Review round 3 (lead decisions; §8.9.2, §8.9.3 and §10.5):
  - The borrow-more branch now runs even when the initial borrow rounds to zero. `residual-zero-borrow` returns incoming `[2, 1]` at a 48 kHz asset / 16 kHz source. Source-rate vectors and property picks add 8, 16 and 22.05 kHz. All 2,096 old input results stay unchanged; the broader picks change 1,617 property inputs and 1,570 results. The guard-only change alters neither old nor expanded property results.

Both Core property suites check every condition. `refuse-word-below-source-sample` and `refuse-final-word-clamp-collapse` pin both validation paths; the clamp case's word spans more than six source samples before clamping.

The Media filter `CaptionParityTests|CaptionPipelineTests|CaptionGalleryTileTests` passed all 11 tests in 3 suites, including all 3 gallery tests (114 old-path overlays, 152 Core-sample overlays and the cancellation race guard). Both full test builds with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` passed: the clean build flagged only the existing CLIProcessRunnerTests, ClipDomainTests, CaptionFixtureSupport, MediaPipelineTests and DebugTranscriptPerformance expressions; the incremental final build flagged none. Windows `dotnet build VoxClip.CrossPlatform.slnf -c Release -nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false` passed with 0 warnings and 0 errors. Running the Core test DLL directly reported 1,543 total, 1,515 passed, 28 skipped (22 Windows-only, 5 live opt-ins and 1 informative caption case), 0 failed. The Media `NativeShapingLayoutAndTimelineMatchEveryFixture` filter passed 86 of 86. Each Core has 6 gallery tests and replays all 86 vector cases.

### 2026-10-27 — Caption gallery tile sample in Core (opus-caption-gallery) — round 1; amended by round 2 above

- Implements captions §15.2–15.3 and design §6.1 "Tile content" in Core, with the same API as Windows. `Sources/VoxClipCore/Domain/Captions/CaptionGallerySample.swift` adds `CaptionGallerySampleSource` (`page`, `preview`), `CaptionGallerySample` (`source`, `words: [CaptionInputWord]`, `time`, `duration`, `make(project:scope:playhead:style:wordsVisible:resources:)`, `preview(_:)`) and `CaptionGalleryPages` (`init(project:scope:style:wordsVisible:resources:)`, `sample(at:)`). All are pure. `make` is the rule and samples a fresh `CaptionGalleryPages` once. Preparing plans the scope and normalises every caption word: in a debug test build, 19 styles over a 2,000-word take took 1.2 s, 0.12 s of it planning. So the apps keep one `CaptionGalleryPages` per style while the transcript, edits, captions, scope and Settings switch stay the same, and call `sample(at:)` as the playhead moves; a lookup scans the pages only.
- The rule (§15.3). The scope's caption copy is normalised and covered (§3.1, §4.4) and paged by §6.1 in the style resolved at the export frame, with `captions.overrides` only for the selected style's tile since round 2 (a rejected patch is ignored), without the §6.2 fit. Pages are visible on their §8.1 intervals over the scope's edited duration. The sample is the page visible at the playhead, else the next page (a gap, or before the first page), else the last page. Inside the page's active word `a`, `time = 0.4·a + 0.35·0.98`; otherwise it is the caption card's drawn moment for that page, `drawsAtLastWord` included. `preview.text` (split at spaces, ids `preview-{i}`) stands in when the scope has no caption words or `wordsVisible` is false, with `time = 0.4·a + 0.35·min(progress ?? 1, 0.98)` and `a = min(activeWord, n − 1)`. A playhead that is not finite counts as 0. Words carry the caption word ids, the stored text (corrected, no transform) and no emphasis, so each style's own emphasis rule applies.
- Reuse, not copies. The internal `CaptionCardText.ScopeWords` is the card's former first half (style resolution, `VoiceLabOutputPlanner.build`, normalisation and cascade, weak words), plus `card(source:words:)` for the key word and drawn moment. `CaptionCardText.make` now uses it and still passes all 51 `cards.json` cases. The internal `CaptionLayoutEngine.pageEnd(start:lastEnd:next:layout:duration:)` is §8.1 moved out of `build` unchanged. `FixedAdvanceShaper` in `CaptionCardTests.swift` is now internal, so the gallery tests share it.
- Vector: `shared/fixtures/captions/gallery-samples.json` (`voxclip-caption-gallery/1`) has 72 cases over 8 projects (7 `preview`, 8 in clip scope), sha256 `1cb9476133311eb97e9c8152c3e00133a39c894261b756e04a0f404ebc7d78e9`. It covers a playhead inside a word, between words, in a page's hold, in a gap between pages, and before the first and after the last page; cuts inside a page (edited 0.9 s in the interview take lies inside "today", source 0.9 s inside the cut "um,"); two clip scopes; corrected words; words hidden; no transcript and every word cut; the 1-word and Line overrides; a digit key word; and Estonian diacritics with weak leading words. The styles are vox-highlight, karaoke, active-pop and typewriter, plus all 19 at two playheads on the r1d capture's take. `tools/captions/gen_gallery.py` (standard library; Node 22.13 for `card_plans.mjs`) wrote identical bytes on two runs and under Python 3.14, 3.13 (with the project schema check) and 3.9. It refuses a word the style's primary font does not cover whole, and a sample that the tile's timing would split or that is not visible at its time.
- Generator refactor, with no fixture change. `captionref/takes.py` now holds `gen_cards.py`'s project builder, schema check and `card_plans.mjs` runner; `card.py` exposes `entry` and `is_weak`; `text.py` imports `regex` only for grapheme clusters, and its standard-library abbreviation test agreed with the old `regex` pattern on 36,012 strings; `card_plans.mjs` also returns each scope's edited duration. `gen_fixtures.py` and `gen_cards.py` still reproduce the committed fixtures byte for byte.
- Tests: `Tests/VoxClipCoreTests/Captions/CaptionGallerySampleTests.swift` has 5. They replay every vector case; sample every case from one `CaptionGalleryPages` per project, scope, style and switch (30 reuses); lay every sample out with `FixedAdvanceShaper` and check that the word at `time` is the active word, with and without Reduce Motion; and check NaN and infinite playheads and the preview default progress.
- "Preview unavailable" root cause (Mac Media, test first), in `Tests/VoxClipMediaTests/CaptionParity/CaptionGalleryTileTests.swift`. `tilePathRendersEveryStyle` makes `CaptionHandoff.tile`'s two calls as it makes them: `CaptionPipeline.layout(words:…)` with no emphasis, then `CoreTextCaptionRasterizer.rasterize(…, time: 0.2, previewWidth: 320, reduceMotion: true)`. It covers 19 styles, 3 frames (the capture's 1280×720 Source frame and both presets) and 2 word lists from the shared interview take: the capture's state ("So" cut, "um," corrected: `corrected today we ship uh the`) and "So" itself corrected (`corrected um, today we ship uh`). Neither call throws, and all 114 overlays are non-empty, so no Media or Core function is wrong. The nil comes from `CaptionHandoff.tile` (`CaptionHandoff.swift:72`), whose `catch { return nil }` (line 105) also turns `CancellationError` into nil.
- The order of events. The correction changes `EditorModel.captionTileInput` (`EditorModel.swift:560`; `sampleWords`, `state` and `plan` all change), so `.task(id: input)` (`InspectorView.swift:97`) cancels the running task for every tile and starts a new one without waiting. The cancelled run fails at its next check (`CaptionLayoutEngine.build`, or `renderBand` in the rasterizer), and `tile` returns nil. Line 99 then writes `image = nil` and line 100 `unavailable = true`, which shows "Preview unavailable" (line 94). All 19 tiles share one `CaptionPipeline`, one `CoreTextShaper` and one `CoreTextCaptionRasterizer` actor, which do not promise to run the two runs in order. If the cancelled run resumes after the new run has written its image, its nil is the last write and nothing reruns the task, because `input` no longer changes. Even in order, it shows "Preview unavailable" until the new run finishes, and longer when that run is cancelled in turn. A run cancelled while in `generator.image(at:)` takes the `try?` and returns an older image instead. `cancelledTileRunResumesLate` replays this deterministically with the real calls: a run parked before its layout, and one parked between layout and raster, are cancelled; the newer run draws; then the older run resumes and throws `CancellationError` in both cases.
- App fix, left to the Mac redesign track (App sources untouched here): `tile` rethrows `CancellationError` (`catch is CancellationError { throw CancellationError() }` before the catch-all), `CaptionTile` ignores the result of a cancelled run (`guard !Task.isCancelled` after the await, before writing `image`), and a tile that cannot render draws exactly the backdrop it would have had (the band of the current frame dimmed 35%, the card backdrop with no frame, stage black in the loading states), never text (captions §15.2, design §6.1; amended by round 2). It also adopts `CaptionGalleryPages` for the sample words and time, replacing the six words after the playhead, the fixed 0.2 s and the "Your story" fallback (`CaptionHandoff.swift:76`, `EditorModel.swift:562`).
- Regression guard: `coreSampleRendersEveryStyle` renders the Core sample through `CaptionPipeline.layout(input:style:)` and the rasterizer at `sample.time` for 19 styles, both presets (9:16 and 16:9), and 4 scenes: inside "corrected", after a phrase, after the last page, and no transcript. All 152 overlays are non-empty.
- Verification on the tree at base `468df1ca`, with `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, `VOXCLIP_LIVE_TESTS=0` and `VOXCLIP_TEST_DOWNLOAD=0`: `swift build` passed. `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 542 tests in 37 suites, with 8 live opt-ins skipped. `swift test --filter 'CaptionParityTests|CaptionPipelineTests|CaptionGalleryTileTests'` passed 11 in 3 suites. A test build with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` flagged only five existing expressions, none in this change.



### 2026-10-27 — Rewrite length budget and Fix with AI (opus-vl-rewrite-budget)

- The lead's brief for track opus-vl-rewrite-budget decided these in-place Core changes on 2026-10-27 (voice-lab §8.1 path 3 and §8.9.1 "Length", "Rewrite request" and "Check"; plan.md ADR-R30 follow-up items Prompt, Request and Contract fixture). The lead's round-two decisions of the same day added the strict JSON reply rule, the Claude Code alias rule, no rewrite fallback model, the full-request estimate, the prompt-hash binding, malformed edits, deletions and ordinal keys (voice-lab §8.1 and §8.9.1, ai.md §2.1 and §12.8). The round-three decisions placed the strict JSON rule in ai.md §4.5 for every reply and transport envelope, and added one exact NFC, dated snapshot ids and one preflight for both sends (ai.md §2.3, §4.3, §4.5, §5, §6.1, §6.8, §6.9 and §12.8; editing §4.8; voice-lab §3.1, §8.1, §8.9.1 and §10.2). App and CLI have no callers of the changed or new types; only Core tests changed with them.
- `Domain/VoiceLab/VoiceLabTextLength.swift` adds `VoiceLabTextLength.length(_ text: String) -> Int`: the number of scalars of general category L* or Nd after NFC (`EditingPrimitives.nfc`).
- New `EditingPrimitives.nfc(_:) -> String` is the one exact NFC of editing §4.8. Foundation's `precomposedStringWithCanonicalMapping` leaves a precomposed LV syllable before a trailing jamo (U+AC00 U+11A8) as two scalars, and after decomposition it also composes U+11A7 and U+11C3, which are no trailing consonants. So `nfc` decomposes and composes run by run between those two jamo; it matched Python's NFC on 860,799 probe strings, where the precomposed form alone missed 11,571. `CaptionRules`, `TightenValidation`, `EditingImport`, `EditSession`, `ProjectValidation`, `ShareTextRules`, `ShareDisclosure.raw`, `PatchRewriteValidator`, `VoiceLabAlignment` and `VoiceLabTextLength` use it; main's `ShareDisclosure.nfc` is gone. Text holding that Hangul now normalises as NFC does.
- `VoiceRewriteRequest.init(id:scope:sourceWords:instruction:language:)` → `init(id:scope:sourceWords:instruction:language:targetSeconds:minChars:maxChars:)`, with `targetSeconds: Double`, `minChars: Int` and `maxChars: Int` as required members. Outside 0 < `targetSeconds` ≤ 600 (finite) and 1 ≤ `minChars` ≤ `maxChars` ≤ 5,000, `estimate`, `prepare` and `propose` throw `VoiceLabError.invalidInput` before a quote or a send. The quote fingerprint covers all three.
- `VoiceRewriteProposal` gains `proposedLength: Int` (the `VoiceLabTextLength` of `proposedText`) and `inRange: Bool`. A proposal outside the range is still returned as it is. Its `model` is now the model that answered.
- `VoiceRewriteService.requestText(_:source:prompt:)` (internal) renders every request string from `request` in `shared/prompts/voice-rewrite.json`, now `promptVersion` 1. A missing template or placeholder throws `VoxClipError.sharedResourcesMissing`. The user's request is sent after `jsTrim`, and its block is left out when it is empty. `propose` validates the input before it checks the quote. The rewrite text may hold no control character (Cc) other than a line break; before, only C0 controls were refused.
- `Domain/VoiceLab/PatchRewriteContracts.swift` adds `PatchRewriteLineRange(line:minChars:maxChars:)`, `PatchRewriteRequest(id:entries:ranges:instruction:language:)` with `entries: [ClipWord]`, `PatchRewriteQuote` (`id`, `requestID`, `connector`, `model`, `estimate`), `PatchRewriteAuthorization(quote:approved:)`, `PatchRewriteEdit` (`line`, `text`, `reason`, `length`, `inRange`, `checkThis`, `isDeletion`) and `PatchRewriteProposal` (`id`, `edits`, `unknownLines`, `duplicateLines`, `malformedItems`, `checkThisMessage`, `connector`, `model`, `providerRequestID`).
- `Services/VoiceLab/PatchRewriteService.swift` adds `public actor PatchRewriteService`: `init(shared:)`, `estimate(_:connector:) -> CostEstimate` (operation `patchRewrite`), `prepare(_:connector:model:) -> PatchRewriteQuote` and `propose(_:connector:model:authorization:) async throws -> PatchRewriteProposal`. It keeps at most 32 quotes, and a quote is used once. The send is `LLMFeature.voiceRewrite` with medium effort, schema `patch_edits` and tag `patch-rewrite`, so the HTTP connector makes one attempt. `Domain/VoiceLab/PatchRewriteValidation.swift` holds the internal `PatchRewriteValidator` with the §8.1 rules: an edit whose `text` or `reason` holds a Cc other than tab, or whose `text` is over 2,000 UTF-8 bytes, is counted in `malformedItems`; an empty `text` after `jsTrim` is a deletion; labels, keys and digit runs compare as scalar sequences. Kept edits over 20,000 bytes of text together throw `LLMError.invalidOutput`.
- Both rewrite services: the quote binds the prompt file as the SHA-256 of its bytes after CRLF→LF (new internal `VoiceLabLLMChecks.readPrompt`). The estimate is `est()` of `system + cachedBlock + instruction` with the catalogue's token policy, unit `token`, basis `unknown`, and `estimate` checks the request first. A reply must come from the quoted model by the ai.md §12.8 rule (`VoiceLabLLMChecks.answeredBy`: a Claude Code `sonnet`, `opus` or `haiku` accepts `claude-<alias>-…`, `default` any `claude-…`; an HTTP connector also accepts `<selected>-YYYY-MM-DD` or `<selected>-YYYYMMDD` naming a Gregorian date, through the new `isSnapshotDate`; ids compare as UTF-8 bytes), else `LLMError.invalidOutput`; then `.truncated` and `.refused(category:)` as before.
- Both rewrite services share one preflight in `VoiceLabLLMChecks` (ai.md §12.8): `isValidModel` takes 1 to 256 UTF-8 bytes with no Cc (before, any non-empty id up to 256 bytes); `maxOutputTokens(sourceTokens:promptCap:connectorCap:)` is now also checked by `prepare`, which throws `LLMError.contextTooLong` (before, only `propose` checked it); `requiredTokens` is `est()` of the joined text and compact schema, plus the cap and 64 (before, two `est()` and no 64); and `contextFits(context:required:safetyFactor:)` needs `context ≥ 512` and `required ≤ ⌊context × factor⌋` (before, `required < Int(context × factor)`).
- `CanonicalResponseParser.extract(_:)` keeps its signature but now reads Unicode scalars and parses strictly (ai.md §4.5): a one-line code fence leaves nothing; each parse needs one RFC 8259 value of at most 64 levels with no unpaired surrogate escape and only finite, non-underflowing numbers. The new internal `StrictJSON` (`Domain/Clips/CanonicalValidation.swift`) checks the whole text and builds the `JSONValue` itself, with a stack of open containers instead of recursion: numbers through `Double(String)`, so an exact zero with an exponent such as `0e1` is zero where `JSONDecoder` refused it, strings from the checked escapes, and a repeated key's first value. `strictDecode` no longer calls `JSONDecoder`. `validate(_:schema:)` refuses a connector-parsed object deeper than 64 levels through the bounded `StrictJSON.nests(_:deeperThan:)`. `CLIConnector` extracts CLI reply text with the same function, omits `--fallback-model` for `LLMFeature.voiceRewrite`, and `HTTPWire` sends no Anthropic `fallbacks` or beta header for it.
- New internal `CanonicalResponseParser.envelope(_:)` (a `String` or `Data`) is ai.md §4.5's transport envelope: jsTrim, then one strict parse of at most 512 levels, with no code fence or balanced-object scan. HTTP response bodies (`HTTPConnector`), the model cache (`HTTPModels`), Claude auth output and the Claude result envelope, and Codex exec and app-server lines (`CLIConnector`) all use it, in place of `JSONDecoder` or the reply extraction. Before, `JSONDecoder` read them with its own number, escape and repeated-key rules; it refused `0e1`, for example. CLI output now splits into lines at LF only (before, at every newline character, U+2028 included).
- `shared/prompts/patch-rewrite.json` is new, at `promptVersion` 1; rule 5 allows deleting a whole line with empty text (sha256 `696abde9b8d87378d286e754ff57155b659114f8d76db2a6f1f8b07dcb2def0a` after CRLF→LF). `voice-rewrite.json` is at `promptVersion` 1 (sha256 `f981955d21d7bbd739c90c5da3597997a315fb8bc503b9f1f83f486d85d4f4c0`).
- Fixtures, all generated and replayed by both suites:
  - `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/text-length-cases.json`, 36 cases, sha256 `f3366ba2658ef7d1a047e492310ed046d03e912892c58387e54b41c053544b48` (`tools/fixtures/voice-lab/generate_text_length.py`);
  - `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/contract.json`, now 11 `rewriteMutations` rows with `targetSeconds`, `minChars` and `maxChars`, sha256 `5cc2ebfc8ab91d685e322e7e644fc47f69915c1cdc115c74368b5a8ade6182b9` (`generate_contract.py`, which reproduced the earlier hand-written file byte for byte);
  - `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/patch-rewrite-cases.json`, 72 cases, 35 of them also replayed through `VoiceRewriteService`, sha256 `f81a6c42c45a48813d3444c66509847e012e1748f988ad8f5c6d1d204756cbb6` (`generate_patch_rewrite.py`);
  - `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/rewrite-checks-cases.json`, 37 answering-model, 16 model-id, 12 output-cap and 12 context rows, sha256 `7b9bf5a0a7ce9b7ed5caecf2d1981d824dc70b67298542490bc928361351d998` (`generate_rewrite_checks.py`);
  - `shared/fixtures/llm/voice-rewrite-requests.json`, 7 cases, sha256 `d7c527d83edb38e156c34dc93f471a086edad66390a6f09406e2fd04ab666201`, and `shared/fixtures/llm/patch-rewrite-requests.json`, 5 cases, sha256 `cce0daae2220a9bef4a738c30d92effa4f3ddf5b266892a2049610fd3f460e3f`, each case with `expected.estimate` and `expected.preflight`; `shared/fixtures/llm/cli-replies.json`, 17 Claude result envelopes, sha256 `0f8c451b2bf1d6ae06b2154162ab151ddfc013e325b54f06f79f97f1365914de` (`npm --prefix tools/fixtures/llm run generate`);
  - `shared/fixtures/editing/ingest/clean-words-unicode.json`, sha256 `3ab3506bad92a4236bd354f094395316386c5d616ced8358d497e5aa91b32654`, with U+FFFE and Hangul LV+T, U+11A7 and U+11C3 (`tools/fixtures/editing`; the other 85 fixtures are unchanged).
- Tests: `Tests/VoxClipCoreTests/VoiceLab/VoiceRewriteBudgetTests.swift` and `PatchRewriteServiceTests.swift` are new; `CapturingRewriteConnector` takes an optional connector, answering model, stop, model list and context. Both services replay the 37 shared answering rows (`onlyTheQuotedModelMayAnswer`), and `sharedRewriteChecks`, `outputCapIsCheckedWhenTheQuoteIsPrepared` and `quoteWithoutAPriceAsksForConfirmation` (the quote reaches `VoiceLabCostPresentation`'s no-price confirmation) are new; both request runners check `expected.preflight` at the smallest context and one token below. `HTTPResponseTests.responseBodyIsOneStrictEnvelope` (7 bodies) and `CLIConnectorTests.sharedClaudeResultEnvelopes` (17 envelopes) are new; `AISharedFixtureTests` lists `cli-replies` and leaves its `cli-reply` kind to the CLI suite. `VoiceLabServiceTests.swift` carries the budget, `CLIConnectorTests.swift` checks that a rewrite names no fallback model, and `ReviewRegressionTests.swift` that it sends no Anthropic beta header or `fallbacks`. `SharePostTextTests` uses `EditingPrimitives.nfc`. On the tree merged with main `196b25c7`, `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 560 tests in 39 suites and `--filter CaptionParityTests` passed 4.

### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab pricing, cost presentation and spend ledger (opus-vl-pricing)

- The lead's decisions for voice-lab §3.1 and §3.2, with a typed hook for §3.3 live prices, revised after review rounds 1 to 3. This is an in-place Core change. The approval flow, quote fingerprints and request bodies left by wave2-vl-request-bodies are unchanged. `ElevenLabsVoiceLab` records each accepted action itself. App and CLI need no wiring: neither constructs the service, and no App or CLI path calls a Voice Lab provider outside it. The Settings key test only sends the free `GET /v1/user/subscription`, and transcription goes through `ElevenLabsTranscriber`, which is not a Voice Lab action. Old → new:
  - `ElevenLabsVoiceLab.estimate(_:units:unit:) throws -> CostEstimate` → `estimate(_ operation: VoiceLabOperation, modelID: String?, units: Double, candidates: Int = 1) throws -> CostEstimate`. It prices from the catalog with the discovered credit multiplier.
  - Both `ElevenLabsVoiceLab` initializers gain `spend:`. The production `init(keys:region:logger:prices:spend:)` takes `spend: VoiceLabSpendLedger = VoiceLabSpendLedger(locations: DataLocations())`. The session initializer takes `spend: VoiceLabSpendLedger? = nil`, so a test or another caller with its own transport records nothing unless it passes a ledger.
  - `VoiceLabReceipt` gains `spendWarning: VoiceLabSpendWarning?`, and `VoiceLabAudioResult.spendWarning` and `VoiceLabCloneResult.spendWarning` forward it. Deletion returns the receipt itself.
  - The private `send` takes the approved action as `billing:` and records it on the reply's 2xx status line (round 4, below). The call sites no longer record.
  - A complete 2xx reply is still kept when the task is cancelled after its last byte, and a cancelled non-2xx reply still throws `cancelled`. The internal test seam `runAfterLastByte(_:)` runs a hook at that point.
  - A lip sync of zero seconds is invalid input to `VoiceLabPricing.estimate`, and `kind` confirms every lip sync, even at $0.
  - Both `ElevenLabsVoiceLab` initializers gain `prices: VoiceLabPriceCatalog? = nil`. Nil loads `catalog/voice-lab.json` through `SharedResources.locate()` on first use; a missing or defective catalog is `sharedResourcesMissing`. Main's Media test `VoiceLabSourceAudioTests` builds the service without `prices:` and prepares a conversion, so it now reads the shared catalog. CI sets `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, and the suite passes on the tree merged with main `f777c433`.
  - Quotes carry catalog estimates. TTS counts Unicode scalars with the unit `character` (was `characters`). The voice changer counts WAV seconds, frames / rate, with the unit `second` (was 1 `call`). Creating or deleting a voice is one free `call`.
  - Model discovery refuses a `character_cost_multiplier` that is null, a boolean, a string, or a number outside 0.01–100 (`malformedResponse`). A missing or null `model_rates`, or one without the multiplier, means 1.
  - Main's paid-asset helpers moved from Media `VoiceLabAssetStore` to Core as `package enum VoiceLabPaidRoot`: `open(_:)`, `directory(_:in:)`, `fullSync(_:)` and `Failure`. The ledger therefore opens `<data>/VoiceLab` exactly as the asset writer does. The asset store maps `Failure` to its own errors for the file. Its behaviour is unchanged, and its test now calls `VoiceLabPaidRoot.fullSync`.
- New public API:
  - `VoiceLabPricedOperation(provider:operation:endpoint:unit:)` with `.elevenLabsSpeechToText`, `.elevenLabsAudioIsolation`, `.elevenLabsVoiceChange`, `.elevenLabsTextToSpeech`, `.elevenLabsForcedAlignment`, `.elevenLabsCreateClone`, `.elevenLabsDeleteClone`, `.syncsoLipsync` and `.falLipsync(endpoint:)`.
  - `VoiceLabPriceCatalog(data:) throws`, with `format`, `Formula`, `Entry`, `entries` and `entry(for:model:)`, plus `VoiceLabPriceCatalog.load(shared:)`.
  - `VoiceLabPricing.estimate(operation:model:units:candidates: = 1, characterMultiplier: = nil, catalog:) throws -> CostEstimate`, `applying(_ price: VoiceLabProviderPrice, to:) throws -> CostEstimate` and `characterMultiplierRange` (0.01...100). The hook uses `VoiceLabProviderPrice(usd:credits:basis:)` and `VoiceLabProviderPriceBasis` (`provider-estimate`, `provider-pricing-api`).
  - `VoiceLabCostPresentation` with `Kind` (`.inline(String)`, `.confirm(String)`), `kind(_:isLipSync:providerName:cap: VoiceLabMonthlyCap? = nil)`, `format(_:)`, `buttonTitle(_:takes:kind:)` and `capWarning(cap:providerName:)`.
  - `VoiceLabMonthlyCap(limit:spent:)` with `isCrossed(by:)`.
  - `VoiceLabSpendTotal(usd:unknownCount:)`, and `VoiceLabSpendDocument` with `months`, `init?(data:)`, `month(of:in:)`, `total(provider:month:)`, `record(_:month:) throws` and `encoded()`.
  - `VoiceLabSpendLedger(locations:timeZone: = .autoupdatingCurrent, lockTimeout: = .seconds(5))` and `(url:timeZone:lockTimeout:)`. It offers `month(of:)`, `readTimeout`, `read() async -> VoiceLabSpendDocument?`, `total(provider:at:) async -> VoiceLabSpendTotal?`, `record(_:at:) async -> VoiceLabSpendWarning?` and `wouldCross(cap:provider:estimate:at:) async -> Bool`.
  - `VoiceLabSpendWarning`: `replacedCorruptLedger(backupFileName:)`, `lockTimedOut`, `cancelled`, `writeFailed` and `invalidEstimate`. The lead accepted `cancelled` in round 3.
  - `spendWarning` on `VoiceLabReceipt`, `VoiceLabAudioResult` and `VoiceLabCloneResult`.
  - `SettingsDocument.voiceLabMonthlyCap(for:)` and `setVoiceLabMonthlyCap(_:for:) throws`.
- Rules, all pinned by the fixture:
  - **TTS dollars** come from the model's own list price: characters × candidates × price / 1000. The discovered multiplier is a credit multiplier: credits = ceil(characters × candidates × multiplier × credits per character).
  - **Recording:** acceptance is the 2xx status line, because the provider has billed by then. As soon as a 2xx status and headers arrive, `send` starts recording the approved quote's estimate once, in a detached task off the service's actor and the main actor.
    - The record is never undone, and a cancellation after that point does not prevent it.
    - If the body then fails, through a cancellation mid-body, a dropped connection or `responseTooLarge` (by `Content-Length` or by the buffered size), the record still finishes before the error is rethrown. The user sees the error and gets no receipt, but the money is counted.
    - A refused authorization, a failed request, a 4xx or 5xx reply and a cancellation before the status line record nothing.
    - The warning is logged (`voice-lab`, `warn`) and returned as `spendWarning` when the action succeeds. It never fails the action. Because the service's records ignore cancellation, `cancelled` reaches only direct callers of `record`.
  - **Copy:** a $0 estimate is inline with empty text, its button is just the action, and the ledger does not record it. Lip sync always confirms instead. A sub-cent confirmation reads "This will cost less than $0.01 on your {provider} account." An action that crosses the cap always confirms, with the cap sentence appended.
  - **Ledger locking:** `record`, `read`, `total` and `wouldCross` are nonisolated async methods. They poll a nonblocking `flock` with `Task.sleep`, so no thread is ever blocked. Writers take the lock exclusively for up to 5 s; readers take it shared for at most 0.5 s. Only `EWOULDBLOCK` is waited for: any other failure, such as a read-only folder, is `writeFailed` at once, and a cancelled task gets `cancelled`.
  - **Ledger storage:** the root is created through `VoiceLabPaidRoot`: only `VoiceLab`, with mode 0700, never the data folder. Writes flush the 0600 temporary file with `F_FULLFSYNC`, rename it, then flush the directory. Months use `TimeZone.autoupdatingCurrent`. A repeated key makes a file corrupt.
  - **Invalid input** for an estimate means:
    - units that are not finite or fall outside 0…1,000,000;
    - fractional characters or calls;
    - a lip sync of zero seconds, with or without an entry;
    - candidates outside 1–3, or more than 1 on a formula other than TTS;
    - a multiplier outside 0.01–100;
    - a unit that does not match the entry.

    A missing entry and an `estimateApi` entry give `usd` nil and the basis `unknown`. `usd` is rounded half away from zero to 10 decimals.
- Shared files:
  - `shared/catalog/voice-lab.json` (18 entries, `voxclip-voice-lab-prices/1`, including the zero `deleteClone` entry) and `shared/schemas/voice-lab-prices.schema.json`, both checked by `tools/schemas/validate.py`.
  - The settings key `voiceLab.monthlyCapUsd.<provider>`, added to `settings.schema.json`.
  - `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/pricing-cases.json`, written by `tools/fixtures/voice-lab/generate_pricing.py`, which produces identical bytes on every run. It holds 247 cases, SHA-256 `5e2adb4f69f52c92933f5e2077712b91710b7fcc6ce3e7848d1b1de975bb62c7`:
    - 117 estimates, including 7 zero-second lip syncs from the catalog rows and 2 named ones, 5 provider prices, 29 formats, 21 presentations, 10 buttons and 6 quotes;
    - 47 ledger cases: the empty file, 2 month rollovers with 11 records (2 of them free), 1 free-only case, 1 concurrent pair of 2 writers × 50 records, 26 existing files (22 corrupt, 5 repeating a key), 10 cap checks and 6 cap sentences;
    - 12 settings values.

    Both Cores replay every case.
- The Mac bundle copies every tracked file in `shared/catalog` (`bundle.sh` `SHARED_DIRS`), so the catalog ships without a per-file list.
- Test support: `SpeechStubHTTP.Reply.stalls` leaves a reply open after its body, as a server that stops sending does, and returns from `startLoading`. A `failWhen` wait blocks the loading thread that every stubbed session shares, so `URLSession.bytes(for:)` never sees the head. A stalled reply also needs a content type, or URLSession holds the head back to sniff the first 512 bytes.
- Verification (round 4), on the tree merged with main `f777c433`, with `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, live tests off and downloads off:
  - `swift build` had no warnings.
  - `swift build --build-tests` with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` flagged no expression in this track's files.
  - `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 537 tests in 37 suites, including the 18-test pricing suite. Its three service tests use the stubbed transport:
    - one record per accepted TTS and voice-changer action, and none for a free clone or deletion, a refusal, a 401, 429 or 500, or a cancellation before sending or before the status line;
    - one record each for a 200 cancelled after its head and first 64 bytes (a stalled reply), a 200 whose `Content-Length` is over the 32 MiB limit, and a 200 cancelled after its last byte through `runAfterLastByte`, which still returns the audio;
    - a call into a read-only data folder still succeeds and carries `writeFailed`.
  - The Media Voice Lab suites ran 56 tests in 7 suites, and 55 passed. `VoiceLabCompositionTests.nonunityFitRefusesBeforeSnapshotAndPublication` compares the shared temporary folder's listing, and it failed while the parallel caller suite wrote there. That test is unchanged from main, and the scratch folders it counts come from other Media Voice Lab code. Run alone, the composition suite passed 21 of 21. `VoiceLabSourceAudioTests` and `VoiceLabAssetStoreTests` passed 20 tests.
  - Checking for cancellation after the last byte failed the status-line test.
  - Round 3: recording before `send` failed the first service test.
  - Round 2: with the lock disabled, the two-writer test failed in 3 of 3 runs.

### 2026-10-27 — Persisted timestamps round-trip exactly (opus-project-dates)

- `Sources/VoxClipCore/Domain/Shared/UTCTimestamp.swift` now reads and writes every persisted timestamp (editing §10.1), and `ProjectJSON` uses its `.utcTimestamp` date strategies. The old encoder (`Date.ISO8601FormatStyle`) truncated binary seconds, and the old decoder (`ISO8601DateFormatter`) returned a `Date` one binary step below the written millisecond. So a load and save moved about 42 % of millisecond timestamps 1 ms earlier, again on every later save: up to −5 ms after 100 saves in the probe. Foundation's Gregorian calendar is Julian before 1582-10-15, so earlier dates were days off the Windows text (10 days in 1581, 2 in year 1). The new code is integer proleptic Gregorian arithmetic.
- Writers round the exact binary value of a `Date` to the nearest millisecond, an exact half going to the later instant, and always write three digits. A date outside years 0001–9999 is an encoding error. Readers accept 0–9 digits and an uppercase `Z` only, and round the same way, so a loaded date is the one the next save writes. `CacheStore.readEntry` (architecture §8.2) and the App's `update.lastCheckAt` check also accept a `±HH:MM` offset.
- The same writer now serves cache `meta.json`, `SpeechRawCache` entries (were `.iso8601` whole seconds), model `installed.json` (was `ISO8601DateFormatter`), `llm-models.json` `fetchedAt` (was `ISO8601Format()`), the Apple speech locale ledger, provenance sidecars (Media; `export.createdAt`, `generatedAt` and consent `verifiedAt` were whole seconds) and settings `update.lastCheckAt` (`AppModel.swift`, was `ISO8601Format()`). The App's 24-hour check reads that setting with `UTCTimestamp.date(from:allowingOffset: true)`, because its old `ISO8601DateFormatter` refused fractions, and settings validation already accepted both forms. It also serves `voxclip-cli find-clips --json`, whose `createdAt` was a number of seconds since 2001 from a plain `JSONEncoder`.
- Left alone: log lines; the ElevenLabs clone label, a provider request body sent in whole seconds and pinned by `request-cases.json`; HTTP `Retry-After` dates; dates that are never persisted.
- `shared/fixtures/projects/timestamps.json` holds 602 cases (317 texts, 53 refused texts and 232 instants) and a 10,000-instant SplitMix64 digest. Its SHA-256 is `321a85bda3e68fbc669a07e3d94ae738686b92c6a0012591d9c992401fcf852e`, and `tools/fixtures/projects/generate_timestamps.py` writes it byte-identically on every run. `Tests/VoxClipCoreTests/Editing/TimestampTests.swift` replays every case through `UTCTimestamp`, `ProjectJSON` and `JSONProjectStore`, checks the digest, and saves every example project twice.
- The two example projects and the ten Voice Lab examples still store whole seconds, so their first save writes `.000`. `TimestampWireRule.nonCanonicalExamples` lists them. For the same reason, `portableProjectRoundTrip` and `VoiceLabUpgradeTests` compare saves with the examples' timestamps written in canonical form.
- `ElevenLabsTranscriber.transcribeWithCache` never overwrites a paid entry. Before it writes its placeholder, `SpeechRawCacheEntry.setAside(_:unlessPlaceholderFor:)` moves any file that is not this key's placeholder (undecodable, another key or another schema) to `<name>.unreadable-<unix ms>`, never replacing an existing file. `SpeechRawCacheEntry` decodes `createdAt` leniently: a missing or foreign date falls back to the file's modification date, so a date-format change alone cannot make a paid response unreadable. Tests: `unreadableEntryIsSetAsideBeforeANewUpload` and `foreignCreatedAtStillServesThePaidResponse` (`SpeechCacheSafetyTests`).
- Cache metadata whose instant rounds past 9999-12-31T23:59:59.999Z fails `CacheStore.readEntry`, so the entry is a cache miss that extraction rewrites and eviction removes, and nothing throws. `CacheEnvelopeTests.metadataPastTheLastMillisecondIsAMiss` pins it.
- Tests pin the new writer text: the sidecar's `export.createdAt` (an exact half millisecond, written `.063Z`), `aiSegments[].generatedAt` and `consent[].verifiedAt` (`VoiceLabDisclosureTests`); the three-digit form of `installed.json` `installedAt` (`ModelStoreTests`), `llm-models.json` `fetchedAt` (`HTTPModelTests`; `HTTPHarness` takes an optional `cacheURL`) and the raw entry's `createdAt`; and `update.lastCheckAt` in both forms (`EditingSettingsRecovery`). The index below lists `UTCTimestamp` and the `.utcTimestamp` strategy extensions.
- Compatibility: macOS builds from before 2026-10-27 read raw entries, `installed.json` markers and the Apple speech locale ledger with `.iso8601`, which refuses fractional seconds, so they treat the files this build writes as missing. Such a build also overwrites an unreadable raw entry with its placeholder before it uploads again, so running it after this one can bill an ElevenLabs transcription twice. Windows builds from before that date read the new text.
- Verification after merging main `ca4064f8`, with `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, live tests off and downloads off: `swift build` passed with no warnings; `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 483 tests in 33 suites; `swift test --filter VoiceLabDisclosureTests` passed 5. `swift build --build-tests` with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` flagged seven expressions, none in a file this track changed. Two generator runs wrote `timestamps.json` byte-identically.

### 2026-10-27 — Shared share-text rules and disclosure line in Core (share-core)

- Implements publishing.md §2.1 and voice-lab.md §11.3 in Core, with the same API as Windows. `Domain/Share` is pure: `ShareDestination` (`x`, `linkedIn`, `voxConvo`), the internal `ShareTextRules`, and `ShareDisclosure` (`removedWarning`, `shareAnyway`, `restoreLine`, `xMadeWithAITip`, `isPresent(_:line:)`, and the internal `fieldText(_:line:rules:)`). `Services/Share/SharePostText` (`preflight()`, `limit(_:)`, `xWeight(_:)`, `linkedInLength(_:)`, `voxConvoLength(_:)`, `composerText(_:destination:disclosureLine:)`, `defaultCaption(title:words:)`, `fieldText(_:line:)`, `capCaption(_:)`) reads `shared/data/share-text.json` once through `SharedResources` and passes the rules to Domain, so Domain stays I/O-free. The public field text was `ShareDisclosure.fieldText(_:line:)`, a Services extension that loaded the file behind a Domain name; it is now `SharePostText.fieldText(_:line:)`, as on Windows.
- Fails at launch, never mid-session: a missing or invalid file throws `VoxClipError.sharedResourcesMissing`, as C# does, and the load result is kept for the process. The functions that need the file are `throws`. `AppModel.init` calls `SharePostText.preflight()` after `SharedResources.locate()` and maps a failure to `startupError = .sharedResourcesMissing`; voxclip-cli runs it in `CLIExecution.run` before every command (help and `version` excepted). After a successful preflight nothing throws. `SharedResources.locate()` accepts a root only when it has `caption-styles` and `data/share-text.json` (architecture §2.4).
- NFC is Foundation's plus the Unicode §3.12 Hangul LV + T composition it omits (`ShareDisclosure.nfc`: U+AC00 U+11A8 is U+AC01). `xWeight`, `composerText`, `isPresent` and the line search use it through a share-local `raw`; `CaptionTextRules.raw` is unchanged.
- `fieldText` drops trailing White_Space, appends the line after a blank line and, when that passes 3,000 scalars (`share.captions`), cuts the caption, never the line, with the composer rules measured in scalars (voice-lab.md §11.3). A rule-4 cut never ends inside a grapheme cluster (a Prepend scalar and its space). `isHost` scans only host characters. The composer bisects the rule-4 candidates, and the rule-5 cuts except for X, each measured whole.
- X rule 5 is `lastFittingXCut`: prefix sums of per-scalar weights and one sweep for URL starts (`urlStarts`) weigh every first-word cut in one pass, from the longest down. It picks the cut a scan of whole candidates picks; publishing.md §2.1 gives the argument. It needs the ellipsis and the trailing punctuation to stand alone, so `ShareTextRules(data:)` now rejects a file whose ellipsis is not one trailing-punctuation scalar, or whose trailing punctuation is not ASCII or General Punctuation, is White_Space or emoji, or joins a letter, an emoji ZWJ sequence or a virama in one cluster. This replaces the BMP check.
- Worst case before, on the main actor: the first word with `.` or `:` was scanned from the longest cut down, each cut measured whole. At 3,000 scalars, in an optimized build (`swiftc -O` harness over `ShareTextRules.swift` and `ShareDisclosure.swift`), it took 431 to 678 ms: `a.` + 2,998 `a` 678 ms, `今日は12:30に…` 489 ms, `VoxClip1.0で…` 490 ms, 1,500 × `a.` 470 ms, `data:` + 2,995 `A` 431 ms. At 10,000 scalars it took 7.45 s. After: 0.9 to 1.4 ms, 4.1 ms at 10,000, with the same outputs. The bisected 3,000-scalar captions went from 1.3–1.6 ms to 0.9–1.4 ms. In the debug test build, the seven 3,000-scalar captions of `spaceFreeCaptionsCutQuickly` take 0.3 s together. `xFirstWordCutsMatchTheLongestFirstScan` compares the one-pass cut with the scan on 120 random first words (75 cut, 19 keeping a URL).
- Shared data: `shared/data/share-text.json` (sha256 `3a620cb39601457e39e7a665e44accece9580d18754267caf6bbab9279b1ead0`) holds the twitter-text v3 weights and ranges, the limits 280, 3,000 and 2,048, the URL weight 23, 29 TLDs, the trailing punctuation, the ellipsis and 81 Emoji_Presentation ranges from Unicode 15.1 `emoji-data.txt` (sha256 `d7aef489c8fe4c14f09ea5695200277c6b93ac82ac60845cdd2161b0d6835cc1`). `bundle.sh` ships `data` as a seventh runtime `shared/` folder, and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md lists the file under the Unicode Character Database. `scripts/test_release.py` reads `SHARED_DIRS` from `bundle.sh` and checks that it covers every shared folder the Swift sources read.
- Fixture: `shared/fixtures/share/cases.json` has 76 cases, 12 default captions, 8 line combinations in en and et, and 10 language codes; sha256 `e505f150e903ee0e970c92450a33a81f164a59ae2a42c5af6f0726fd91879522`. The 15 `word-*` cases pin X first-word cuts, among them URLs that come and go, ports, trailing runs, e-mail, `data:`, times, emoji and Prepend. Two have leading White_Space before a first word that starts with U+0301: `word-leading-white` (the mark joins a space, so the word starts inside a cluster) and `word-leading-mark` (the mark is a cluster of its own after CR LF). `mark-on-space-first-word` keeps the earlier `word-leading-white` text, which rule 4 cuts after a first word of one U+0301 on a space. The first 60 cases are byte-identical. `generate_share_cases.py` (standard library) scans every cut literally, asserts that its one-pass `x_first_word_cut` picks the same cut for every case and 60 random first words, and asserts that every `word-*` case is cut inside its first word for X (`check_case_rules`). It wrote identical bytes on two runs. Swift `Character`s match every fixture cluster, and outputs are compared as UTF-8 bytes.
- `VoiceLabDisclosurePlan.postText` is Estonian when the transcript language's primary subtag is `et`, compared case-insensitively; before, any `et` prefix matched.
- App: `ShareText.weight` forwards to `SharePostText.xWeight` and is nil only after a failed launch check, which hides the X counter; Open X shows the error in the sheet's notice. The X intent text is `composerText(_:destination: .x, disclosureLine: nil)`; percent-encoding and the host check are unchanged. `ShareCaptionDraft.load` and `edit` cap the draft with `SharePostText.capCaption`, its first 3,000 Unicode scalars, as the Windows field now does, so the field, Copy caption, both composers and the saved caption see the same text; `commit` no longer caps. `openShare` copies the caption and reveals the files at once, builds the composer URL in a `Task.detached`, and opens it back on the main actor. While one is in flight, `opening` ignores further clicks and disables Open X and Open LinkedIn. The default caption (`EditorModel.shareCaption`), the removal warning, the X tip and the field prefill are left to the redesign tracks.
- CLI: `cliRequiresReadableShareTextRules` runs voxclip-cli with `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR` on a copy of `caption-styles` and `data` in the run's own `cliDirectory()` (`shared`, beside `VOXCLIP_DATA_DIR`), so overlapping runs never touch each other's copy. With the copy intact, `probe` of an absent file exits 3 with `mediaMissing`. With `share-text.json` missing, and then containing `{`, it exits 3 with `sharedResourcesMissing`, while `--help`, `--version` and `version` exit 0. `runCLI` takes the `sharedRoot`.
- Verification: `swift build` (Core, CLI and App) with zero warnings. After merging main at `217cd334`, `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 500 tests in 34 suites (11 in `SharePostTextTests`), and `cliRequiresReadableShareTextRules` passed with `cliInputFailuresUseJSONAndExitThree` and `cliVoiceLabPlanRejectsActiveAndPreservesDisabledSource`. `test_release.py -k resource` passed 5. A test build with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` flagged nothing.

### 2026-10-27 — opus-speech-concurrency — Fair app-wide speech queue per cache entry and clock-free timing tests

- Hosted CI run 40458357425 (main `f3153d5`) failed `applicationWideConcurrencyLimits`: all seven jobs hit its 5 s saturation deadline while one local and one cloud job ran. The test used `SpeechJobGate.shared` alongside the ElevenLabs and paid-cache suites, which run in parallel. Those all use the synthetic content key and plan, and the gate key (`contentKey + rawKey`) left out the data folder, so they shared one key and kept one of the two cloud slots busy for 8–15 s on the hosted runner; even passing hosted runs took the test 5–11 s. A probe holding one shared cloud slot for 6 s reproduces the failure exactly.
- `SpeechJobGate` (transcription §13.4) no longer polls every 50 ms. Waiters queue in arrival order, and a release hands the slot straight to the oldest waiter whose kind has room and whose key is free, so a later caller cannot take it first (the old gate let the later caller win 20 of 20 probe rounds) and a hand-off takes microseconds instead of up to 55 ms. A waiter never holds back the other kind or another key. A cancelled waiter leaves holding nothing; a slot handed over just before a cancellation stays with its job, which releases it as before. `snapshot()` reports the counts and the waiting keys for tests.
- `TranscriptionService` keys the gate by the path of the raw result entry, `<data>/Cache/<contentKey>/transcripts/<engine>-<model>-<language>-<digest8>.json`, and language detection by `<data>/Cache/<contentKey>/language-detection`. Identical jobs (same source, settings and data folder) still run one at a time, and the second reads the first one's entry. Jobs in different data folders, such as the parallel suites above, no longer share a key.
- transcription §13.4 now states the per-entry rule both apps follow (Windows runs a C# port of this gate, `SpeechJobQueues`): identical jobs run one at a time, a job starts at once when its kind has room and no identical job is running, a waiting job holds no place, and a finished job's place goes to the oldest waiting job of its kind whose identical job is not running. The fast path in `acquire` keeps this. `acquire`, `release` and `cancel` each leave no waiter that could start, because `release` rescans every waiter in arrival order, so a caller that finds room and a free key passes only waiters whose identical job is running.
- Known gap, older than this track: §13.4 says waiting local jobs show "Waiting for the current transcription…", but neither app reports a waiting state. `TranscriptionStage` has only preparing, uploading, transcribing and aligning (`Transcriber.swift`; `TranscriptionModels.cs` on Windows), and `SpeechJobGate.acquire` suspends without a progress report. This track does not build it.
- `TranscriptionService`'s public initializers share `SpeechJobGate.shared`; the internal `init(locations:catalog:gate:)` takes a private gate for tests.
- `applicationWideConcurrencyLimits` runs seven services on a private gate and holds each job in its engine until the test finishes it. At every settled state each kind runs exactly as many jobs as its limit and the remaining jobs allow, and each release starts the oldest waiter of its kind. `identicalJobsWaitForEachOtherOnlyInOneDataFolder` runs two identical ElevenLabs jobs in one data folder and the same job in another: the duplicate waits although a cloud slot is free, the other folder's job starts at once, and the duplicate then comes from the cache. `CountingSpeechEngine` now reports `scribe_v2` for ElevenLabs results, as the real engine does, so they match their cache key. `cancelledQueuedAndFailedJobsGiveBackTheirPlaces` and the `SpeechJobGateTests` suite cover arrival order, kind and key independence, a release that passes over a waiter whose identical job runs, cancelled waiters and a cancellation after a hand-off. None has a wall-clock bound, and with a gate that leaks a slot they fail at the one-minute suite limit instead of hanging.
- `escapedPipeHolderCannotBlockTimeoutOrCancel` drops its 6 s wall-clock bound, which it failed on hosted run 40379391363 and in two track verifications here; it checks that the escaped holder, now sleeping 300 s, is still alive when the run ends. `spawnedChildHasEmptyBlockedSignalMask` gives the `/usr/bin/python3` shim 50 s instead of 5 s; the whole test took 2.5–3.7 s on hosted runs and 0.1 s here, and one hosted run timed out. `cliWaitForFile` no longer gives up after 200 polls.
- Verified after merging main at `ca4064f8`, which includes `95e7b98`, with `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR`, live tests off and downloads off. The test build added no warnings, and a test build with `-warn-long-expression-type-checking=300` flagged no expression this track wrote. `applicationWideConcurrencyLimits` passed 200 of 200 runs. `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 478 tests in 33 suites, 8 of them opt-in or informative skips. With the gate key back at `contentKey + rawKey`, `identicalJobsWaitForEachOtherOnlyInOneDataFolder` fails at the one-minute limit. In round 1 (base `f3153d5`) the old test also passed 50 of 50 runs, alone and alongside the ElevenLabs and paid-cache suites, on this 14-core host, which is too fast to keep a cloud slot busy for 5 s; a gate that leaked the local slot failed that round's five new tests in 124 s. Round 3 changed only Windows; on this Mac `applicationWideConcurrencyLimits` then passed 50 of 50 runs again, and the full suite passed 478 tests in 33 suites, 8 of them opt-in or informative skips.

### 2026-10-27 — Review fixes before merge (opus-mac-redesign round 1d)

- **The window and keyboard focus.** Attaching a project and the start of a video import call `makeKeyAndOrderFront`, which also un-minimises the window, so a project or video opened from Finder or Open Recent while the main window is closed brings it back. `openProject(at:)` does this before anything else, so its refusal notice is visible too. The transcript takes keyboard focus when the editor shows (`TranscriptFocusOnShow` in `EditorView.swift`, where main's `EditorWindowSetup` did it). It always does so for a newly opened or imported project, and a take without a transcript keeps the request until its transcript arrives. When the selection leaves Home it does so only if the sidebar list doesn't have focus, so the arrow keys keep moving through the rows.
- **Versions browser.** A version (`NSDocument.isInViewingMode`) gets its own `VersionWindowController`, with its own `WorkspaceShell` and the sidebar hidden. `replaceProject(with:)` refuses versions, and `EditorModel.replace` and media relinking refuse to change them. This needs a manual check, because a saved project with versions can't be scripted: File > Revert To > Browse All Versions… should show each version in its own window, the main window should keep its project, and leaving the browser should close the version windows. The self-test checks a version forced with `versionForTesting`.
- **Make Clip** composes `.addClip` and `.setActiveClip` through `EditHistory` into one `replace` ("Make clip from selection"). It announces "Clip N made.", and the selected row scrolls into view (`ScrollViewReader` on the sidebar list). If an existing clip already covers the selection, Make Clip selects that clip and announces "Clip K already covers these words." Keep, Dismiss and Remove on the selected row move the selection to the next row in the same undo step: the next kept clip or suggestion, else Full Take. On a row that isn't selected they leave the selection alone. Find Clips selects its first new suggestion, in transcript order, in the same undo step. When the sidebar is hidden, Find Clips shows it for the setup callout and for a run's progress.
- **Sidebar column.** The sidebar list sits in a `ScrollViewReader`, so `.navigationSplitViewColumnWidth(min:ideal:max:)` moved onto that root view. Left on the list inside it, the modifier was ignored and the sidebar shrank to about 144 pt.
- **Selection.** If the editor refuses a row change, for example while the video is missing, the selection returns to the editor's scope. Recent rows are selectable (`SidebarItem.recent`): the arrow keys reach them, and Return or a click opens the project, or Locate… when its video is missing or unreadable. Selecting one keeps Home showing. An inline rename ends when the field loses focus or when the selection or project changes, and it keeps the typed title. Escape keeps the old title.
- **A running import** refuses opening, closing and replacing the project with "Finish the current task first." The check sits in the leave boundary, ahead of its no-project shortcut, so opening from Home is refused too. A `fromImport` flag lets the import replace the project itself.
- **The title rule** is `WorkspaceShell.projectTitle(_:fileURL:)`, used for the window, the sidebar header and Home: `Project.title`, else the file name, else the video's name, else "VoxClip". As a result, Save As, Move and Rename change the file and the proxy icon but not the title, and the save-boundary checks now assert this. If a Rename should retitle the window, Save As and Rename would also have to write `Project.title`. That question is open for the lead.
- **Narrow widths (design §3.7).** Below a 960 window the sidebar collapses, and it comes back at 960 and wider. The narrow-width rule uses the sidebar's measured width, which the user may have dragged up to 320. The transcript measure is never wider than the pane less its 84 gutter, so a narrow pane wraps instead of clipping.
- **The right column width** is stored only after a drag of the editor split's own divider. `EditorSplit`, an `NSSplitView` subclass, receives `mouseDown` only on or beside its divider, because its two panes cover the rest of it, and `super.mouseDown` returns when the drag ends. The width is stored when it changed and the column isn't collapsed. This replaces the `NSApp.currentEvent` test, which also stored a width after a toolbar click or a drag of the sidebar's divider. The self-test drives a drag by posting mouse events in an offscreen window.
- **Help and the Dock.** Help > Keyboard Shortcuts (⌘/) and Workspace Tour work on Home and with no project open. `showShortcuts` moved from `EditorModel` to `WorkspaceShell`. The sheet lists the current bindings: ⌘1 / ⌘2, ⇧⌘K, ⌥⌘I and ⌃⌘S. The Dock icon shows the main window whenever it is hidden or minimised, including while Settings is open.
- **The transcript column** now tests `EditorModel.transcribed`, an observed flag set by `refresh()`, instead of `project.transcript`, which views don't observe. A transcript that arrived without another observed change, or an Undo of "Transcribe video", used to leave the column showing the wrong state.
- **Sidebar details.** An untranscribed take reads "Not transcribed", and both actions carry the tooltip "Transcribe the video to select words." (C7). Keep is a bordered 24×24 control (`KeepButtonStyle`). A focused selected row draws its meta in full `alternateSelectedControlTextColor` instead of the delta's 88%, so the 11 pt text reads at 4.90:1 on the dark accent selection. The Home AI callout's glyph is `text.secondary`. The native sidebar geometry stays, with text at x 336 instead of the mockup's position. This is an intentional HIG deviation (lead answer 1).
- **Export** stays the painted Petrol capsule until a real-window check (lead answer 2). Its title, reasons and enabled state come from `project`, which views don't observe, so the real-window scene audit caught it still reading "Export Video…", disabled, after Make Clip selected a clip. It now reads `EditorModel.projectRevision`, which every refresh that finds the project changed bumps. Its title also sets its accessible name. The same capture showed the label without its fill in the key window, because the button redrew only when SwiftUI updated it. It now also redraws when its window gains or loses key status and when the app becomes active or inactive. A later unlocked run shows the Petrol capsule in the key window, with the label measured from the captures at 4.66:1 in dark and 5.49:1 in light. The inactive-window state has no real-window capture yet. Export Clip… in a clip row's menu calls `presentExport(clipIDs: [clip.id])`, so it exports that clip without moving the selection.
- **The edit summary's detail** has no tooltip (lead answer 3): "{source} source · {w} words cut in {r} places · {p} pauses cut" (`EditorModel.summaryDetail`). The lead asked for it as the subtitle's accessibility value, but AppKit's titlebar text field always reports its own text as its value and ignores `setAccessibilityValue`, as a standalone check showed. So the detail is the field's accessibility help, which VoiceOver reads after the subtitle. `describeSubtitle(in:detail:)` finds the titlebar text field whose text is the window subtitle and reapplies the help whenever the subtitle or the detail changes.
- **Sidebar caption cards** give a boxed word the style's own box colour. The 45% lift is gone (lead answer 4; the lead amends design §5.4.3).
- **CaptionHandoff card additions** are lead-approved under the contract path: `card(_:)`, `drawCard` and the 8 MB card LRU are additive, and `layout` and tile behaviour are unchanged.
- **Poster JPEGs** go through `ThumbnailCache.posterFolder(in:contentKey:create:)`. It checks the entry and its `thumbs` folder with `CacheStore.isRealDirectory`, a new public nonisolated static that `CacheStore.isDirectory` now calls. An entry or `thumbs` that is a link or a file skips the disk cache. Missing folders are made one level at a time, never through a link, and a poster that is a link is never read or replaced.
- **The Five questions fixture** is still generated in DEBUG (`DebugSidebar.fiveQuestions`). It is now paced per paragraph, with the pauses after the first three paragraphs cut, so the step 8 checks assert the checklist's literal values: "2:10 after cuts · 8 cuts", "Clip 2 · 0:20 after cuts · 2 cuts" and "Clip 4 · 0:11 · No cuts yet". The shared fixture under `shared/fixtures/` is still a step 18 deliverable.
- **Harness.** The window-server capture check skips, with the reason, when the console is locked, when the process may not record the screen, or when `screencapture` could not run. Round 1's window-server path never produced an image: `screencapture` refuses a destination file name that starts with a dot and still exits 0. The temporary file is now named `<capture>-window-server.png`. An unlocked `-sceneAudit` run now captures the real sidebar in a real window, in light and dark. The selected row's selection is visible and readable there, in its unfocused form, because the transcript has focus. The focused accent selection has no real-window capture yet, so lead note 3a stays open for the lead to close. `-sceneAudit` covers the new layout again: Find Clips readiness in its popover (including the late stale status), Make Clip, Transcribe Again's warning and Cancel, Export from the toolbar, 1024 wide, opening from Finder into a closed window, focus and the Dock with Settings open. A popover check that fails while the process isn't a trusted accessibility client is reported as skipped. In the unlocked run all of them passed. The UI integration tests in `MacUIIntegrationTests` are opt-in (`VOXCLIP_UI_TESTS=1`). `documentScene` now expects at least 46 results, accepts skipped ones and still needs its twelve captures.

### 2026-10-27 — Right column width setting

- `SettingsDocument.defaults` gains `ui.rightColumnWidth` (null by default). Validation accepts null or a number from 240 to 720, matching `shared/schemas/settings.schema.json`. The App clamps the value to its own column range and stores it only after the user drags the divider. The key belongs to the ADR-R29 editor layout, and both redesign tracks use it.

### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab patch window geometry (wave2-vl-seams)

- Added pure Core geometry for a re-voiced passage in `Domain/VoiceLab`. `VoiceLabPatchConstants` carries the §8.3 constants this geometry uses, plus `VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL`, the binary64 value of 10^(−45/20), and `RMS_FRAME`, the 10 ms envelope frame. `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.place(project:scope:envelope:firstWordId:lastWordId:)` places a replace window for a `Project` and a `VoiceLabOutputScope`, as `VoiceLabOutputPlanner.build` receives them, and returns a `VoiceLabWindow` with bridges, seconds, samples and one `VoiceLabSeamPlacement` per side. `VoiceLabHandles` adds `minimum`, `seam` and `checkTrim`; `VoiceLabAlignment` adds `words`, both `trim` forms and `relativeWords`; `VoiceLabRoomToneSelection.select` picks room tone. Refusals are typed `VoiceLabGeometryError` values. The `afterWordId` overload returns `insertionUnsupported`. No caller, provider request or render path uses these functions yet.
- Round 3 makes every window that `place` returns pass `VoiceLabProject.validate` and `VoiceLabRenderContract.prepare` in its scope (voice-lab.md §8.4, §10.5):
  - Kept source comes from the editing builders the output planner uses (`detectPauses`, `EditState`, `clipIndices`, `clipTimes`, `buildSegments`, `buildEditPlan`), with the window's words and pauses dropped, so nested items, cut pauses and a clip's head and tail count. This replaces round 2's `words:cutWordIds:sourceSampleRate:durationSeconds:` overloads and their neighbouring cut-word reach. A gap frame qualifies only when its centred fade lies wholly in kept source, measured against the widest window the other side could reach.
  - Take edges count kept items only. With no kept item before the window, it starts at sample 0 with seam `none` and absorbs earlier cut items; the end mirrors this at exactly `durationSeconds`. The rule holds whatever the fade, and in a clip scope only where `clipTimes` reaches the edge. A hard seam that rounds onto an edge no longer becomes it.
  - After placement, a seam with a fade needs `floor(N/2)` kept samples before it when incoming and `ceil(N/2)` after it when outgoing. Otherwise the window fails with `seamFadeCrossesCut(incoming:needed:available:)`, which replaces `seamFadeCrossesCut(String)`. A window shorter than both inner half fades, the validator's `overlappingFades` condition, fails with the new `windowTooShort(needed:available:)`. A hard seam's reach is clamped at zero beside overlapping items; without the clamp the property fuzz found an untyped `invalidInput`.
  - `words` refuses a token that holds a noncharacter (U+FDD0–U+FDEF, U+xFFFE, U+xFFFF) with `alignmentMismatch` before NFC, as the Windows Core and the reference do.
- Round 4 keeps that guarantee in a scope that already holds enabled replacements (voice-lab.md §8.4 steps 4 to 6, §10.5, §15 test 3):
  - `place` selects the scope's enabled replacements as `VoiceLabOutputPlanner.build` does, each with the fades of its selected recipe. Kept source drops the words and pauses inside every replacement as well as the window's, and removes every synth range. Replaced words never bridge and block a take edge, and a gap frame must leave each replacement's outer fade halves free.
  - A window that overlaps a replacement throws the new `overlapsReplacement(String)` with its segment ID. The new `adjacentReplacement(String)` covers a window that touches a replacement, leaves an outer fade half or the kept piece between them too short, or drops items that lengthen a cut's reach until another replacement's fade crosses a cut or two replacements touch. A replacement that crosses the clip, has no synth samples, has no selected recipe or overlaps another gives `invalidInput`. `place` never emits `synthToSynth`.
  - Step 4 now follows §8.4: the window absorbs cut items, events and unlabeled speech wholly inside it, and refuses only an item that crosses a seam or a kept word outside the passage and its bridges (`windowCrossesWord`).
  - `VoiceLabGeometry.check` applies the project transcript rule (finite times, `0 ≤ start < end`, starts in order, unique IDs) to `place` and to room-tone selection.
  - `VoiceLabLoudness` (wave2-vl-audio-math) and `VoiceLabConform` now read `VoiceLabPatchConstants.VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL` and `RMS_FRAME` instead of their own −45 dBFS constant, `pow` call and 0.01 s literals. `VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL` now has one definition on this platform; the 10 ms frame does not. The code that builds the envelopes `place`, loudness and conform read still uses its own 0.01 s: `EditingImport`, the `AudioEnvelope` default in `TranscriptContracts`, `CacheStore`, Media's `AudioPipeline` and, since the main merge, `VoiceLabSourceAudio`. Each must equal `RMS_FRAME`, and changing `RMS_FRAME` alone would not change how envelopes are built. Those files belong to other tracks. Behaviour is unchanged: `pow(10, −2.25)` is the same binary64 value on this libm.
- Round 5 follows the round-4 review (voice-lab.md §8.4 steps 1, 2 and 6, §10.5, §15 test 3):
  - An edit after acceptance, such as cutting the pause under a replacement's seam, no longer blocks every later window with `adjacentReplacement` for that replacement. `place` now checks each replacement's seams and pair rule on the kept pieces with the window and without it, in the new private `SeamLayout`. A check that holds without the window and fails with it throws `adjacentReplacement`, as before. A check that already fails is judged by prepare's own rule: the neighbouring output piece is source and holds the fade's outer half, and the kept source between it and the next replacement, the window included, holds both outer halves. If that rule holds with the window, the window is accepted. If it holds only without the window, `place` throws `adjacentReplacement`. If it fails without the window too, the scope did not render before the window, and `place` throws `invalidInput(segmentId:)` with that replacement's ID. The window's own neighbours get the same check.
  - `VoiceLabGeometryError.invalidInput` became `invalidInput(segmentId: String? = nil)`, and every other refusal is `invalidInput()`. Only Core and its tests use the type.
  - When no gap frame fits, a hard seam beside a replacement R measures its silence from `(R.endSample + ceil(N_R/2) + floor(N_hard/2)) / rate`, and the right side mirrors it with `floor(N_R/2) + ceil(N_hard/2)`. Re-voicing the sentence right after an accepted one is then accepted whenever the silence holds both outer halves: the review's 1.203 s case gets a hard seam at sample 57,696, 816 samples after the replacement, and validates and prepares. With less room the touching or pair rule still refuses it with `adjacentReplacement`.
  - `cutAfterAcceptanceRecipeValidatesAndPrepares` replays the review's reproduction on the passage fixture: after `pause-after-w8` is cut, w1 is placed at 0 to 98,160, and the project prepares as synth, source, synth, source.
- `VoiceLabWindowTests` consumes all 599 shared `window-cases.json` vectors (schema `voxclip-voice-lab-window-cases/3`, sha256 `5c758d8445230581f8455c365d0becea9c1faf2f77814fc6ba3e8797819f60de`, from `tools/fixtures/voice-lab/generate_window.py`). It decodes them with `JSONDecoder` into Core's `JSONValue`; `JSONSerialization` returned an inexact `NSDecimalNumber` for 17-digit values and dropped U+FEFF. Each window vector carries a take, which the suite builds on the passage fixture without its own replacement, the take's replacements, which it builds as one-word passage recipes, and its scope.
  - The 300 `propertyCases` sample a 20,000-case fuzz from LCG seed 20261027: 12,204 windows accepted, as in round 4, none failed the validate and prepare model, and every refusal was typed. The 150 `multiSegmentCases` sample a 20,000-case fuzz from seed 20261028. Its takes first receive one or two replacements from `place` (11,650, of them 926 disabled), and 5,228 takes then receive edits after acceptance: cut items beside a replacement, cut pauses under its seams and replacements enabled again. 8,329 windows were accepted (1,988 directly beside a replacement, 1,578 after later edits) and none failed the model. Every refusal was typed; the 53 `invalidInput` refusals that name a replacement all come from takes that fail the model without the window.
  - A scratch harness, outside the repository and since deleted, replayed all 20,000 single cases and all 40,515 multi-segment placements (every replacement placement and every final window, after the later edits) through this Core and the Windows Core. Every outcome matched the reference, and all 32,183 accepted windows validated and prepared with every replacement in the project.
  - Every accepted case now validates and prepares beside the take's replacements: 64 window vectors, 186 property cases and 63 multi-segment cases. A case whose take holds room tone selects the expected room tone and builds a one-word passage recipe; the other 34 window vectors, the §10.5 edge vectors among them, are built as voice-changer recipes, which carry no text, words or room tone.
  - Round 5 adds named vectors for a pause cut under an accepted replacement's seam (a window elsewhere is accepted, and the next sentence gets a hard seam 70 ms after the cut), a gap seam whose fade would overlap that replacement's across the cut (`adjacentReplacement`), a window that moves a pause cut into a far replacement's fade (`adjacentReplacement`), cut words that leave a 5 ms sliver after a replacement (`invalidInput` naming it) and a window over those words, two replacements already too close for both fades (`invalidInput` naming the first), the pair rule and touching on each side, and hard seams that clear a replacement's gap or hard fade on each side, at 44.1 kHz too, where the odd hard fade pins which half each side keeps.
  - Six recipe tests also pass both. They cover gap seams with bridges, after the fixture's own overlapping replacement is refused; the sentences before and after an accepted replacement, with all three prepared together; a 44.1 kHz whole take with `none` seams at 0 and at an off-grid `durationSeconds`; a window beside a cut word; a cut filler at 0.0 s absorbed at the take start; and the review's pause cut after an accepted replacement. A recipe with one short handle is refused.
- Lead decision 4 keeps every v3 TTS word `synthetic: true`. The public type index lists this track's types, but older `Domain/VoiceLab` types are still missing from it.
- `swift build` passed with zero warnings. `swift test --filter VoxClipCoreTests` passed 488 tests in 33 suites; the 8 existing live and informative tests were skipped. No provider call, VM or push was used; main 51dac9d was merged into the track branch only.

The oracle tests spell it `\u{41}`, and the GitHub case `gh\u{70}_`, so the repository secret scan no longer matches the fake keys. The decoded cases are identical.
